# 15. Email and SMS

## Email and SMS Strategy, Economics and Program Planning

### Why Email and SMS Matter

#### Return on Investment

- E-commerce companies earn roughly $40 in revenue for every $1 spent on email (older, 2021-22). Email is therefore treated as a high-performing channel. [L0414.040, L0609.001]
- Sending promotion emails costs almost nothing beyond the email tool itself. [L0623.007]
- A cited survey (older, 2021-22) claims 89% of marketers see email as their main lead generation channel and that email wins more customers than Facebook and Twitter combined. Email matters most for a new business that still needs to grow its list. [L0462.022]
- Email is one of four parts of the funnel, alongside Google Shopping ads, organic search and retargeting ads (older, 2021-22). [L0458.048]
- Email marketing is part of the marketing system alongside paid search and Google Shopping. Organic search traffic is included as a traffic source (older, 2021-22). [L1481.022]
- Email complements retargeting as another way to stay in front of visitors who have not yet bought (older, 2021-22). [L1239.012]
- Email and retargeting together keep prospects engaged between their first visit and their purchase, especially those in a longer buying process (older, 2021-22). [L1240.012]
- Once a list has grown, campaigns to it become an additional traffic and sales source alongside paid ads and organic content. This reduces dependence on paid traffic for repeat sales. [L0634.037]
- Email list growth starts slowly and compounds as site traffic rises (older, 2021-22). [L0609.007]
- Email adds touchpoints that build trust, especially before a first purchase. Results compound over time, similar to SEO (older, 2021-22). [L0609.008]

#### Touchpoints and Trust

- A prospect needs on average around seven touchpoints with an e-commerce business before buying. A single visit is often not enough, so ongoing engagement is required (older, 2021-22). [L0414.036]
- Email is the place for ongoing, longer-form communication that delivers knowledge, builds trust, demonstrates authority and builds a unique brand image (older, 2021-22). It should not be used only for repeated product promotions and discounts. [L0414.041]
- Email is a personal channel. Messages should read as if sent from one person to another, because people trust people more than faceless brands (older, 2021-22). [L0609.002]
- Email success depends on how consistently you send, how valuable the content is, and how many emails reach prospects and customers. Software choice matters less; the tool is only a vehicle (older, 2021-22). [L0608.001]
- Many small and medium e-commerce businesses run no email marketing at all. Even a basic setup puts a store ahead of these competitors, since email is a channel cheaper than paid ads for repeat sales (older, 2021-22). [L0609.009]

#### Repeat-Purchase Economics

- A customer's second or third purchase typically costs far less to acquire than the first. Repeat orders are more profitable and generate cash to put back into winning new first-time buyers (older, 2021-22). [L0414.044]
- A repeat sale to an existing customer carries no new acquisition cost, so the whole order value is close to pure profit. [L0435.007]
- Repeat purchases do not happen by accident. They need defined processes and back-end marketing separate from front-end acquisition marketing, aiming to turn first-time buyers into second and third-time buyers and eventually brand advocates (older, 2021-22). [L0414.045]
- Improving the autoresponder sequence raises the value of every subscriber. Higher value per subscriber lets you pay more to acquire the next subscriber and grow faster. [L0378.091]
- Email is one of the main levers for returning customers. Build the list over time and use it for holiday promotions, new products and reminders (2020, older). [L1111.120]
- Good opt-ins and email marketing raise the value of every paid click. In one large client account, email generated about 10% of total revenue linked to paid traffic. [L0510.048]
- Automation effort is low once set up. Automated flows are built once and keep earning every month. Most of the setup runs without ongoing work, except for testing and one step that needs more hands-on effort. [L0572.004]

#### Ownership of the Customer Relationship

- The customer relationship belongs to you, not the platform. You can market to customers indefinitely, and they can return to purchase multiple times. [L0280.026]
- Email retargeting is one of the most powerful tools on Shopify. Customers who give their email address become a permanent marketing asset you can reach indefinitely. [L0280.025]
- Promotions to your email list, plus traffic from several sources, help smooth out random swings in daily and weekly sales (2021, older). [L0671.102, L0671.240]
- Promotions for cold traffic are less effective than promotions sent to a list of past buyers or visitors, who already know the store (2021, older). [L0801.127]

### Campaigns and Flows

#### Definitions

- Flows are automated sequences triggered by visitor or customer behaviour, such as signing up, abandoning checkout, buying or receiving a shipment. They turn interest into revenue without manual sending. [L0605.016, L0573.012]
- Campaigns are one-off sends tied to a date, event or launch, sent to a list or segment. They are not triggered by website behaviour. Flows are the better choice for behaviour-based follow-up. [L0605.016, L0607.017]
- A campaign sends a single email one time to your list. An automation is triggered by a specific event (for example, abandoned checkout) and executes a sequence of steps automatically. [L0282.003]
- In Clavio, flows are automated sequences that start from customer behaviour, such as a welcome series, abandoned checkout and order confirmation. Campaigns are one-off messages sent manually to a chosen audience. [L0635.033]

#### The Rule

- All email campaigns except holidays and major sales events (Black Friday) must be automated flows. Never send one-off campaigns for days such as national pet day. Automation is where volume and leverage come from. [L0041.013]
- Flows are tied to customer behaviour and deadlines. Do not try to make them reactive to news or trends; timely content belongs in campaigns. [L0494.012]

#### Flow Design Requirements

- Every flow needs three elements: a customer behaviour that triggers it, a clear objective, and a deadline. [L0494.013]
- Flow reference table: [L0494.014]

| Flow | Behavior that triggers it | Objective | Deadline |
|---|---|---|---|
| Abandoned checkout | Customer starts checkout | Return and purchase | 72-hour window, then the product goes back on the shelf |
| Welcome series | Visitor wants to buy at a discount (wants a deal) | Get the visitor to buy a product | 72 hours, or until the coupon or discount code expires |

- Flow blueprints are intentionally generic. Their value lies mainly in the gotchas, common mistakes, suppressions and exclusions they describe. Structure and copy still need adapting to each brand before use. [L0588.002]
- A flow for a high-ticket product (for example, a $3,000 dining table) differs fundamentally from one for a low-ticket product (for example, $40 jewellery). Set timing, messaging, offers and sequence length by price point. [L0588.003]

### Which Flows to Launch First

#### Core Flow Set

- Every e-commerce brand needs five automated email flows: a pop-up with a welcome series, an abandoned checkout flow, a browser abandonment flow, an added-to-cart abandonment flow, and a post-purchase flow. [L0494.001]
- The five core flows (post-purchase, welcome series, abandoned cart, abandoned checkout and abandoned browse) generate at least 80% of total flow revenue. They deserve the most attention when building or optimising a store's email setup. [L0588.001]
- Upsells and downsells do not need dedicated flows. Subscribers who receive regular campaigns with new angles and solutions will see upsell offers naturally. [L0494.009]

#### Minimum Launch Set by Source

Sources differ on the minimum launch set. Each row applies to the source named.

| Source | Minimum set | ids |
|---|---|---|
| Source E 2025-26 | Five core flows (welcome series with pop-up, abandoned checkout, browser abandonment, added-to-cart abandonment, post-purchase) | L0494.001 |
| Source C | Welcome flow and abandoned checkout flow at launch; then browse abandonment, post-purchase thank-you, win-back, UGC generator, cross-sell after fulfilment | L0605.017 |
| Source C | Welcome series, abandoned cart, customer thank-you (post-purchase); browse abandonment, cross-sell and win-back later | L0573.013, L0607.018 |
| Source C | Abandoned cart, welcome and post-purchase, built before anything else | L0606.001 |
| Source A (2024, older is not applied) | Abandoned cart sequence and promotional emails first; other topics optional | L0971.098 |
| Source A (2023) done-for-you setup | Welcome series, abandoned cart campaign, post-purchase campaign asking for referrals and reviews, plus Klaviyo technical setup so emails fire at the right times | L0835.097 |
| Source C procedure | Abandoned cart, abandoned checkout and welcome flows in Klaviyo, plus SMS flows with SMSBump for the same triggers | L0693.029 |

- The list of eight flows for an online store, with groups: [L0604.001]

| # | Group | Flow |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Basic | Welcome Flow |
| 2 | Basic | Browse/Site Abandonment |
| 3 | Basic | Abandoned Cart |
| 4 | Basic | Abandoned Checkout |
| 5 | Basic | Customer Thank-you |
| 6 | Basic | Customer Winback |
| 7 | Advanced | UGC Generator |
| 8 | Advanced | Cross Sell After Fulfillment |

- Source A's checklist has six steps. Step one is the abandoned-cart sequence, step five needs more manual work than the others, and step six covers transactional emails. [L0572.005]

#### Timing Against Launch

- Set up abandoned cart and post-purchase email flows before the store goes live, so customers receive follow-up emails from the first sale (2025). [L0667.087]
- Klaviyo must have the abandoned cart flow and the welcome flow set up during store build, before traffic is sent to the store. [L1288.009]
- Start abandoned cart emails from the day the store first gets traffic, because shoppers who leave a cart can still come back (2020, older). [L0468.133]
- Set up welcome and abandoned checkout flows at launch, before traffic is large. Even 50-100 visitors can produce a few extra sales, and missing these flows loses buyers who were already interested. [L0605.018]
- Sources differ on the gate for capture and follow-ups. Build flows before go-live, as L0667.087 and L1288.009 advise. Activate email capture and abandoned cart follow-ups only after live products are on the store, as L1094.104 advises (2023). [L0667.087, L1288.009, L1094.104]
- Set up email from the start for every product, because some buyers take time to decide. It matters most for products priced $50 and above, and for stores with upsell or cross-sell products. [L1218.020]
- Klaviyo should be set up before running ads for a product. Retargeting visitors who did not buy and capturing emails lowers customer acquisition cost by converting more of the traffic already visiting the site. [L0477.014]
- Klaviyo should be set up before any ads run. It turns visitors who already came to the store into buyers, lowers customer acquisition cost, and builds a list for retention and retargeting people who left without buying. [L1297.014]
- Email funnels should be built before scaling other traffic sources. [L0790.067]
- Basic email campaigns are part of baseline store setup, alongside Google Ads, payment processing and remarketing, and should be set up before scaling traffic (2021, older). [L0801.128]
- A new store should have a basic set of automated email flows in place before scaling traffic. These are the minimum requirement for email marketing (older, 2021-22). [L0609.005]
- Newsletter sign-ups and automated flows capture visitors who leave without buying. They give the store a way to reach interested visitors who did not purchase on the first visit. [L1077.051]
- Start sending emails to subscribers as soon as they are on the list, regardless of which opt-in form or source brought them there. Early sends build engagement and begin producing returns while the list is small (older, 2021-22). [L0609.019]
- Email capture and automated sequences should be ready before subscriber numbers rise, so new subscribers get the right email immediately (older, 2021-22). [L0609.007]
- Set up email automations early, because they run continuously in the background. Content marketing can wait until the store has traffic and you know what is working. [L0835.098]
- Email marketing success starts with the basics. Email campaigns form part of the marketing setup before traffic scales. [L0801.128]

### Phased Rollout

Use these phases as the planning order. Each phase starts only when its gate is met.

#### Phase 1: Build Before Launch

- Set up the email sequences in this order: [L0493.015] (older, 2021-22)
  1. Plan the post-purchase and opt-in sequences in advance.
  2. Create any new list segmentations needed to run them.
  3. Build the automations for each sequence.
- Adapt any flow blueprint to your own store: [L0588.004]
  1. Overlay your own branding, tone and messaging onto the blueprint.
  2. Overlay your own thoughts and store data (customer behaviour, price point, product type) onto the blueprint.
  3. Keep the parts that fit your store and discard the parts that do not.
- Build three automated flows in the email service before anything else: abandoned cart, welcome and post-purchase. Together they cover first contact with a new subscriber, recovery of unfinished checkouts, and the second order from existing buyers. [L0606.001]
- Build the email list and flows so that every visitor who opts in keeps hearing from the store. Flows are not needed on the first day for every store, but they become an important revenue source later. [L0551.071] (2020, older)

#### Phase 2: Capture and Launch

- Opt-in and segmentation procedure: [L0414.042] (older, 2021-22)
  1. Map the desired customer touch points from first hearing about the business to being ready to purchase.
  2. At each touch point, offer an option to join an email list or other list (such as push notifications or SMS by phone number). Do not rely on one pop-up for everyone; offer several ways to join across the site.
  3. Segment the list by the touch point where the subscriber entered.
  4. Send contextually relevant communication regularly over at least the first 30 days after joining, moving the prospect through the awareness stages from their entry point.
  5. Do not send the same emails to everyone, because subscribers are at different stages and need different content.
- Implementation can take one to three months. Rolling out the full email setup gradually is acceptable, and consistent starting matters more than speed (older, 2021-22). [L0609.010]
- SMS works alongside email. Set up SMS flows for the same triggers as email flows to recover lost sales, using SMSBump in Source C's setup. [L0693.029]
- Set up only the basic automated emails needed to launch a new store as part of the initial build. Defer advanced email marketing until after the first sales are made. [L0555.011]

#### Phase 3: After First Sales

- Advanced email marketing is a separate, later-stage layer on top of a basic email setup, not a prerequisite for it. [L0544.009]
- Set up email autoresponders for as many messages as possible, not only abandoned carts, so each repeated customer message is sent automatically. [L1515.036]
- Once a list passes 500 contacts, the email program should be profitable with at least about $1,000 in extra sales per month. Check this against the Klaviyo revenue report. [L1076.069]
- Once live, send the holiday promotions to the full email list first. [L1224.015]
- Email lists drive a large share of extra revenue during holidays and monthly promotions. A new store's list grows slowly, mostly from people who opt in through ads, so start building it early (2025). [L1097.039]

#### Phase 4: Scale

- Begin sending email promotions and retargeting campaigns once you have accumulated around 1,000 email subscribers. [L0280.024]
- Advanced email automations are worth implementing once a store is above roughly $100K in sales. Below that level, simpler email flows are enough (2024). [L0965.085]
- Add the advanced flows (UGC generator, cross-sell after fulfilment) once the basic set is running. [L0604.001, L0605.017]

#### Phase Gates

| Gate | Action | ids |
|---|---|---|
| Any list size | Keep sending regular emails; even 200 contacts should receive them | L0390.016 |
| Basic setup live, before scaling traffic | Basic flows in place | L0609.005, L0801.128 |
| First sales made | Begin advanced email marketing | L0555.011, L0544.009 |
| 500 contacts | Expect profitability of about $1,000 extra per month; check the Klaviyo revenue report | L1076.069 |
| About 1,000 subscribers | Begin promotions and retargeting campaigns | L0280.024 |
| Above roughly $100K in sales | Implement advanced automations | L0965.085 |

### Program Management

- Send copies of the emails you want to discuss, or paste their body text, before the call. This lets the reviewer work out improvements in advance rather than reading them during the session (older, 2021-22). [L0364.011]
- Case: a workshop product launched to an email list of about 3,000 people with no prior sales history. The team used reply emails to ask buyers why they bought and to learn prospects' objections. Insights from the replies went into the copy and the offer. In its third month the workshop was generating about $90,000 in revenue from the small list. The team noted that the reply emails provided both the data and the sales. [L0378.090]
- Competitor check: a competitor having no opt-in on its homepage does not prove it skips email marketing. Check content pages and the checkout flow, because some stores email only visitors who take an action such as adding to cart (older, 2021-22). [L0893.014]
- Some e-commerce businesses send no email at all, so an empty inbox after signing up is itself useful information. Record whether the competitor sends abandoned cart emails and any other follow-ups (older, 2021-22). [L0893.015]

### Outsourcing

- Email writing can be outsourced if the operator does not enjoy writing, as long as the full set of planned emails gets built and sent (older, 2021-22). [L0609.011]
- Email writing can be assigned to the same content writer role that produces blog posts, combined with other website copywriting. This frees the owner from writing customer emails and copy (older, 2021-22). [L1334.031]
- A done-for-you email setup typically includes a welcome series for new subscribers, an abandoned cart campaign, and a post-purchase campaign that asks for referrals and reviews. The technical setup in Klaviyo is also included so emails fire at the right times (2023). [L0835.097]
- Find a trustworthy email agency by asking other store owners, and use Upwork only as a last resort. [L1129.010]
- One operator's email list of about 50,000 subscribers produced strong, very profitable revenue through regular sales campaigns run by an email agency. [L1315.043]
- Flow blueprints need adapting before use, so outsourced builds should still be checked against your own price point and data. [L0588.002, L0588.004]

### What to Defer

- Advanced email marketing waits until after the first sales. Add it as a later-stage layer. [L0555.011, L0544.009]
- Flows that matter later, not on the first day, can wait. [L0551.071] (2020, older)
- Win-back and re-engagement do not need separate automated flows. Regular campaigns sent to the full list already reach lapsed contacts. In this context, win-back targets people who have not purchased in two to four weeks. [L0494.007]
- Do not stack extra win-back, re-engagement or upsell flows on top of a regular campaign schedule. This floods subscribers with messages. Keep the automated flows to the core five. [L0494.008]
- Browse abandonment, cross-sell and win-back flows come after the core three are running. [L0573.013, L0607.018, L0605.017]
- Advanced flows (UGC generator, cross-sell after fulfilment) come after the basic set. [L0604.001]
- Optional email topics beyond the abandoned cart sequence and promotional emails can be added later. [L0971.098]
- Content marketing waits until the store has traffic and you know what works. [L0835.098]

### Benchmarks and List Value

#### Share of Revenue

Sources differ on the share. Use each value under the conditions stated.

| Benchmark | Value | Condition | ids |
|---|---|---|---|
| Email share of total online store revenue | 10% to 30% | Generic planning range; depends on the store and how well flows are set up | L0572.001 |
| Email share of total revenue, another source | 10% to 30% | Same range, stated as a planning figure | L1129.010 |
| Email share in one large client account | About 10% of total revenue linked to paid traffic | Revenue tied to paid traffic | L0510.048 |
| Email share of total revenue, one store | About 25% | Store with email funnels built before scaling other traffic | L0790.067 |
| Email share of monthly revenue, one lesson business | About 30% | Part of it from monthly promotions; sending costs almost nothing beyond the tool | L0623.007 |
| Email share of monthly revenue, established store | Roughly 30% | Holiday promotions go to the full list first | L1224.015 |
| Email share of monthly revenue, established store (2025) | About 30% | Treat email as a major sales channel; build the list early | L1287.046 |

#### Revenue Per Month and Per Contact

- At a monthly store revenue of $100, email adds about $10 to $30 per month. At $1,000 monthly revenue, the same 10% to 30% range adds about $100 to $300 per month. [L0572.002]
- An extra $1,000 per month from email over six months adds up to $6,000 in additional profit. Small recurring gains accumulate into large totals over time. [L0572.003]
- The email database should generate at least about $1 of revenue per contact per month (2022, older). For example, 10,000 contacts should bring in roughly $10,000 per month from email with no ad spend. [L0854.079]
- Email generated about 30% of monthly revenue in the lesson's business, part of it from monthly promotions. [L0623.007]

#### List Size Benchmarks

| List size | Benchmark | ids |
|---|---|---|
| 200 contacts | Still send regular emails; subscribers eventually buy. Raising the returning customer rate from 5% to 30% is described as close to free revenue. Email platform setup takes roughly 2 to 10 hours. | L0390.016 |
| 500 contacts | Email program should be profitable with at least about $1,000 extra sales per month; check the Klaviyo revenue report | L1076.069 |
| About 1,000 subscribers | Begin email promotions and retargeting campaigns | L0280.024 |
| About 3,000 subscribers | Workshop case: about $90,000 revenue in the third month from the list, with no prior sales history | L0378.090 |
| About 50,000 subscribers | Strong, very profitable revenue from regular sales campaigns run by an agency | L1315.043 |

#### SMS Benchmark

- SMS marketing acted as a retention tool with a good return on ad spend. For one brand, about 2,000 USD of roughly 50,000 USD in revenue came from SMS. [L1431.050]

#### Other Reference Values

- The core five flows generate at least 80% of total flow revenue. [L0588.001]
- Email is often overlooked but can add 10% to 30% to monthly revenue. [L1129.010]
- A customer's second or third purchase typically costs far less to acquire than their first. [L0414.044] (older, 2021-22)
- Email contacts raise the value of every subscriber, so improving sequences lets you pay more to acquire the next subscriber. [L0378.091]

## Platform Choice, Account Setup and Sender Identity

### Platform Choice, Account Setup and Sender Identity

#### Choosing an Email Platform

- The customer sees only the email itself, plain text or HTML, and every email provider delivers it the same way. The software choice therefore does not change what the customer receives or how they respond. [L0608.005]
- No import tool moves flows, lists and automations from one platform to another, so switching requires manual setup. Factor this rebuild time into any decision to change. [L0608.012]
- Drip and Klaviyo both connect to Shopify and sync customer data for automated email. Choose either one; the choice comes down to preference. [L1298.024, L1298.025, L1298.026]
- Drip is an e-commerce autoresponder that sends automated sequences, such as abandoned cart emails, and syncs customer and order data from Shopify. It is one of two recommended options. [L1298.024]
- Klaviyo is an autoresponder that also covers SMS and forms. It connects to Shopify in the same way as Drip and is a valid alternative. [L1298.025]
- Klaviyo is the most widely used email marketing app for Shopify and is recommended for its strong built-in automations. [L0573.002]
- Klaviyo is the tool used to build the automated flows. An active Klaviyo account is the only requirement to start. [L0606.002]
- Klaviyo is installed on Shopify to send automated emails for bounced visits and abandoned carts. Set it up before the store takes traffic. [L0393.021]
- Send promotions and abandoned cart flows straight from Klaviyo. Add contacts to Shopify only when they buy, become abandoned carts, or sign up through a Shopify-native opt-in form. [L0359.079]
- Clavio is the email and SMS marketing app used in the Source C material for sign-up forms and pop-ups, automated flows (welcome series, abandoned carts, thank-you emails), and email campaigns to the collected list. [L0476.012]
- The Clavio setup is written as the Klaviyo-type setup in the video, so the same steps apply to both. [L0635.020]
- Shopify Email is recommended over Klaviyo mainly because it is much more cost-effective. [L1307.002]
- A store already using Klaviyo with a healthy return on ad spend has no reason to switch immediately. A store that wants to save money should switch. [L1307.002]
- The source claims that, at the time of publication, Shopify Email covered about 99% of what Klaviyo can do. This is a vendor-comparison claim; verify it against current features before relying on it. [L1307.005]
- Shopify Email can replace older email platforms without paid add-ons. It includes AI-assisted features for writing campaigns and images. [L1283.097]
- Ontraport is a marketing automation platform that is expensive but offers many controls and triggers for high volumes, similar to Infusionsoft. [L0923.036]
- Mailchimp is a cheaper option that works well for a standard Shopify autoresponder. Clavio can produce designed email templates. [L0923.036]
- For a pure affiliate site, use GetResponse as the autoresponder. Its free first month lets one affiliate sale pay for the service, and it covers the features needed for affiliate marketing without extra cost. [L0514.023]
- Older advice (2021-22) recommended Drip for all e-commerce emails, including post-purchase, browse abandonment and flows, especially with Shopify integration. Klaviyo remained a decent alternative. Switching was only advisable for new setups, since recreating existing flows is a lot of work. [L0682.016] *(older, 2021-22)*
- Older advice (2020) described Klaviyo as an email marketing app used for email marketing and autoresponders for the stores. [L0852.075] *(older, 2020)*
- Older advice (2021) stated that the newest version of the email setup uses Klaviyo for email capture and does not require Privy. Keeping Privy is fine if it is already installed. [L0853.081] *(older, 2021)*

#### Costs

| Option | Cost as stated | Notes | Units |
|---|---|---|---|
| Shopify Email | Free up to 10,000 emails per month, included in the Shopify subscription | Above 10,000 emails, $1 per additional 1,000 emails. Stated at time of publication; check before relying on it. | L1283.097, L1307.003, L1307.004 |
| Shopify Messaging | 10,000 emails per month free | Each additional 1,000 emails costs 1 USD. | L1317.002 |
| Klaviyo | Free for the first 500 email addresses/contacts, then paid | Billing starts after the first 500 contacts. (Older, 2020, and undated Source A lesson.) | L0268.054, L1076.059 |
| Drip | Costs start at $19 per month | Rises with the number of active people in the account. The account owner decides which people count as active. (Older, 2021-22.) | L1298.028 |
| Drip (Shopify approval screen) | Recurring subscription of $0.00 USD during the 14-day free trial, recurring every 30 days, with a maximum of $7,500.00 USD in possible additional fees | The cap is a buffer so the list can grow without interruption, not a typical cost. (Older, 2021-22.) | L1298.029 |

- Email platforms bill per subscriber, so a new store with zero or few subscribers pays little. Costs rise as the list grows. [L1298.030]
- The post-purchase email flow takes about 5 to 10 hours to create. [L1076.059]

Formula for Shopify Email cost above the free allowance:

```
Cost per month = ((emails sent in the month − 10,000) ÷ 1,000) × $1
Below 10,000 emails per month: $0
Example: 15,000 emails → (15,000 − 10,000) ÷ 1,000 × $1 = $5
```
[L1307.016]

#### Shopify Messaging: Installation and Branding

- Shopify Messaging is Shopify's free app for sending marketing emails and SMS messages from the store. It is used for email campaigns and automations that are set up in later steps. [L1317.001]

Procedure: Install Shopify Messaging [L1317.003]
1. Open the Shopify admin and click Apps.
2. Search for "Shopify Messaging" in the Shopify App Store and pick the best match at the top.
3. Click Install.
4. Accept the access permissions Shopify asks for and click Install again.
5. Once installed, open the app's settings and complete the configuration.

Procedure: Configure Template Branding in Shopify Messaging settings [L1317.005]
1. Open Settings > Template Branding and click Customize.
2. Upload the store logo.
3. Choose the font. The instructor keeps Sans Serif, but any font used on the store can be selected.
4. Set text and background colors. The instructor keeps them black and white.
5. Set the button fill to the store's brand color.
6. Leave the address and store name fields as they are.
7. Click Save.

Checklist after installing Shopify Messaging [L1317.013]
1. Install the app from the Shopify App Store.
2. Set Template Branding (logo, font, colors, button fill brand color) and save.
3. Check the sender email is sales@yourdomain.com and enter the same address under Notification Sections.
4. Keep Double opt-in inactive.
5. Keep Optimize Open Tracking active.
6. Leave UTM defaults unchanged.

#### Klaviyo: Account and Signup Form Setup

Procedure: Install and create the account [L1297.015]
1. Search for Klaviyo in the Shopify App Store, click Add app, then Install app.
2. Enter your sender name and sender email address and click Continue.
3. Select your business goal, here to earn more revenue.
4. Select email only as the channel.
5. Open the dashboard and start with a signup form.

Procedure: Install and connect, as taught in the Source C material [L0573.002, L0573.003]
1. Install Klaviyo from the Shopify App Store. It is registered with the same email address used for the Shopify account. [L0573.002]
2. Connect Klaviyo to the Shopify store. [L0573.003]
3. Turn on website tracking. [L0573.003]
4. Import existing contacts, starting with email contacts and newsletter subscribers. This step can be skipped if none exist. [L0573.003]
5. Optionally build the brand library by uploading logo and brand colors, which Klaviyo then applies to all email templates. [L0573.003]
6. Optionally invite team members. [L0573.003]

Procedure: Initial setup including the signup form [L0477.015]
1. Enter sender name and sender email (for example, a name from the brand and a contact@ address on the store domain).
2. Select business goal (earn more revenue) and channel (email only).
3. Create a signup form via Signup forms in the left menu.
4. Choose a pop-up design, name it and add it to a subscriber list (newsletter).
5. Replace the percentage offer with a fixed dollar offer (for example, save $5 with the first order).
6. Replace the picture with a product or gift image. Free images are available, for example from unsplash.com.
7. On the success step, communicate the discount code created in Shopify.
8. Publish the form.

Procedure: Clavio setup as taught in the video [L0635.020]
1. Go to Apps > All recommended apps, open the Shopify App Store, search Clavio, and click Install.
2. Create the Clavio account and enter a sender name (for example, a support representative's name) and a sender email address on the store's domain (for example, a contact address).
3. Choose email only.
4. Set up core flows: welcome series, abandoned checkout, and order confirmation series. Then use campaigns for one-off sends.

Integration and interface checks
- In Klaviyo's integrations area, check that the store integration is listed as connected. Turning on the site option adds the JavaScript that the sign-up box needs to the storefront. *(Older, 2022.)* [L1092.096]
- When Klaviyo updates its interface, check the current account layout before recording or following lessons. The interface may differ for new accounts even if existing accounts remain unchanged. *(Older, 2022.)* [L0665.086]
- Check that promotional and evergreen sequences are live in the account before relying on them. *(Older, 2021.)* [L1112.077]

#### Drip: Setup Procedure

Procedure: Drip Email Marketing setup [L1298.027] *(older, 2021-22)*
1. In the Shopify App Store, search for drip and click Drip Email Marketing (Klaviyo can be searched the same way).
2. Click Install app. You are taken to the Drip sign-up page.
3. Create a Drip account with first name, last name, email, password and a goal, complete the human check and click Start free trial.
4. Return to Shopify and approve the subscription. The trial starts at $0.00 USD, and then click Start free trial.
5. Check the sender business address that loads from your Shopify account. Edit it in the box if needed, then click Continue.
6. Wait for the account to sync in the background. Confirm that Drip shows the store as connected and that its tracking snippet is installed on the site.
7. Leave the detailed email setup (sequences and flows) for later, when it becomes relevant.

#### Sender Name

- Use a person's name as the sender name, such as a founder, yourself, a customer service rep, or a fictional persona, combined with the brand. For example, "John from ABC Lawnmowers" works better than the brand name alone. [L0572.017]
- In a welcome or flow email, the sender name works better as a named person from the store plus the store name, such as a customer representative, than as a bare brand name. A personal sender name makes the message feel like a reply from a human. [L0476.039]
- Use a named customer representative as the sender name, combined with the store name, and send from the store's own email address. This makes automated emails feel personal and recognisable. [L0634.038]
- The Klaviyo sender name and sender email are requested at account setup and appear on every email. Use a real person's first name as the sender name, so the email reads as a personal message rather than a company broadcast. [L0607.050]
- Set the Klaviyo sender name to a person's first name plus the brand, for example "First name from Brand", with a sender email address on the store's own domain. This gives customers a personal sender they recognise that matches the store. [L1297.045]

#### Sender Address

| Platform or purpose | Address to use | Where it is set | Units |
|---|---|---|---|
| Shopify Messaging | sales@yourdomain.com (sales@ followed by the store's own domain) | Messaging settings, and the same address under Settings > Notification Sections, which is the sender for all Messaging emails | L0147.007, L1317.008 |
| Shopify Email | General address on own domain, such as support@, sales@ or info@ | Settings > Sender email, taken from the store details settings | L1307.008, L1307.009 |
| Klaviyo | Address on the store domain, for example contact@ | Sender email at account setup | L0477.015, L1297.045 |
| Clavio | Address on the store domain, for example a contact address | Sender email at account setup | L0635.020 |
| Store contact and customer service | Shopify support email for store contact and customer service questions; the store's support address for supplier contact fields | Contact and supplier fields | L0964.142 |

- The sender address must be on the store's own domain and never a personal address. [L0147.007]
- Do not leave a personal address such as name@agency-domain as the sender of store emails. Use a sales address on the store's own domain instead. [L1317.009]
- The sender address field in Shopify Messaging can show the email used to create the store, which may be a personal inbox. Replace it with a sales address on the store's own domain before sending any emails. [L1317.014]
- Make sure replies go to an inbox that you or your team can manage. [L1307.009]
- Under Settings > Notifications, the sender email addresses are configured. This is left for later, when email marketing is set up. [L1306.023]

#### Business Domain Email

- Use a business email address on your own domain (name@yourdomain) for suppliers and customers, not a free address such as Gmail, Hotmail or Yahoo. Free addresses make a store look like a small hobby operation, which lowers trust. *(Older, 2021-22.)* [L0566.001]
- Keep business email separate from your personal inbox and accounts. Mixing the two makes supplier and customer correspondence hard to find and manage. *(Older, 2021-22.)* [L0566.002]
- Create one core business address first, such as support@, sales@, hello@ or info@, and use it on the website and for customer contact. Add addresses for specific purposes later. *(Older, 2021-22.)* [L0566.003]
- Do not put a personal or free email address on the website or in supplier outreach. Customers and suppliers expect a domain-based address. *(Older, 2021-22.)* [L0566.004]
- The business address is the one customers use to contact you at launch and the one shown on the website. Set it up before the site goes live. *(Older, 2021-22.)* [L0566.018]
- The personal email address entered during Google Workspace sign-up receives the welcome message and serves as the account recovery address. Use an address you can reliably access. *(Older, 2021-22.)* [L0566.021]
- Google may ask for a phone-number check on a newly created account as a security measure. Creating many accounts in a short time can make this check appear more often. *(Older, 2021-22.)* [L0566.022]
- The verification screen during Gmail activation says the page can be closed and reopened later. Closing the window is not a failed setup. Check the admin console to confirm the activation status. *(Older, 2021-22.)* [L0566.023]
- Pricing differs by source:
  - A Google Workspace search-result snippet at recording time showed plans from $8.40 per user per month. *(Older, 2021-22.)* [L0566.025]
  - The standard cost quoted was about $12 per month. *(Older, 2021-22.)* [L0566.025]
  - Check the live pricing page for currency and region before budgeting, because prices change. [L0566.025]

#### Physical Address and Footer

- Klaviyo asks for a physical postal address to comply with anti-spam rules. Use a registered business address, such as a low-cost London office space or a virtual office, rather than a personal home address. Update the company registration address and the website when the new address is in place. [L0552.040]
- The footer module in the example email library carries the sender's business identity: an ABN (Australian Business Number), a street address and an unsubscribe link. Outside Australia, use the equivalent company registration number and the business's postal address in the same footer block, so every email includes them automatically. [L0610.012]
- Include the business address in the footer of business emails, such as marketing emails. [L0696.013]
- In Klaviyo emails, remove the organization name or full address from the email footer if you do not want it shown. The two sources differ on whether the address is optional: the rule above applies to business emails in general, and this option applies to stores that choose not to display it. [L1297.022]

#### Opt-In Settings

- Choose unconfirmed opt-in when the autoresponder allows it, because subscribers are added immediately with no extra step. Confirmed opt-in sends a confirmation email that many subscribers never see or click, which loses a share of your list. [L0514.024]
- Use a single opt-in with a welcome discount instead of double opt-in, because fewer than 5 percent of subscribers confirm a double opt-in email. [L0857.045]
- Shopify Email: Settings > double opt-in. A double opt-in sends a confirmation email after the customer subscribes and asks them to confirm. Leave the option at its default, "not required", because it adds friction to sign-ups. [L1307.010]
- Shopify Messaging: set Double opt-in to inactive. [L1317.010]
- Configuration for the Messaging app: disable double opt-in, enable optimize open tracking, and leave UTM settings at default values. [L0147.009]

#### Tracking, UTM and Smart Sending

Settings summary

| Setting | Required value | Where | Units |
|---|---|---|---|
| Smart sending | Off, for every flow email (Clavio and Klaviyo) | Email settings before switching to Live | L0476.010, L0477.017, L0573.016, L0634.006, L1297.022 |
| UTM tracking | On, for every flow email | Email settings before switching to Live | L0476.010, L0477.017, L0573.016, L0634.006, L1297.022 |
| Optimize open tracking | On | Shopify Messaging settings | L0147.009, L1317.011 |
| Open tracking (Shopify Email) | Default: track all email opens | Settings > open tracking | L1307.011 |
| UTM parameters (Shopify Messaging) | Defaults: source Shopify email, medium email, campaign UTM campaign, content email name, term activity ID | Messaging settings | L0147.011, L1317.012 |

- In every Clavio email, set Smart sending to off and UTM tracking to on before you switch the email from Draft to Live. The setting window opens when you click the email in the flow. [L0476.010]
- Before a Clavio email goes live, set Smart sending to off and UTM tracking to on in the email settings panel. Then set the email from Draft to Live. [L0634.006]
- For every flow email, make sure Smart sending is off and UTM tracking is on before setting the email to Live. UTM tracking lets you measure the revenue from each email efficiently, which Smart sending would otherwise interfere with. [L0573.016]
- In Klaviyo, set Smart sending to off and UTM tracking to on in each flow email. [L0477.017]
- In Klaviyo emails, Smart sending should be off and UTM tracking on. The flow then sends each message on schedule and the traffic can be tracked. [L1297.022]
- The UTM parameters in Shopify Messaging are appended to links in emails. This lets sales from emails be tracked in Google Analytics and in the Messaging app. Keep them at their defaults. [L1317.012]
- Do not modify the UTM campaign structure or advanced tracking settings in Shopify Messaging. [L0147.011]
- Keep the default option to track all email opens in Shopify Email. This gives the most accurate open-rate reporting for emails sent through Shopify Email. [L1307.011]
- Optimize Open Tracking in Shopify Messaging should be switched on, so email opens are tracked. [L1317.011]

#### Going Live and Final Checks

- An email that stays in Draft is never sent. Each flow email must be switched from Draft to Live after it is edited. [L0476.028]
- Branding and settings changes are not applied unless they are saved. Confirm that all Shopify Email settings are saved before moving on. Otherwise, new automations and campaigns may not carry the intended configuration. [L1307.014]
- Before relying on the Shopify Messaging setup, run the checklist in the Shopify Messaging section above. [L1317.013]

#### Migration Between Platforms

- When moving from Klaviyo to Shopify Email, check your Shopify customer list first. Klaviyo sign-ups usually come through Shopify opt-ins, so many contacts may already be in Shopify and may not need an export. [L0470.078]
- Contacts are added to Shopify only when they buy, become abandoned carts, or sign up through a Shopify-native opt-in form, so a Klaviyo-to-Shopify move should be checked against those routes. [L0359.079]
- Switching platforms requires manual rebuilding of flows and automations. Factor this rebuild time into the decision. [L0608.012]
- Older advice (2021-22) said switching is only advisable for new setups, since recreating existing flows is a lot of work. [L0682.016] *(older, 2021-22)*
- A store that wants to save money should switch to Shopify Email. A store already using Klaviyo with a healthy return on ad spend has no reason to switch immediately. [L1307.002]

## Deliverability Foundations: Authentication, Reputation and Warm-up

### Separate sending domains by list type

- Send existing brand subscribers from the main domain. Send cold purchased lists and re-engagement campaigns from a secondary sending domain or a separate email service, such as Send in Blue, to protect the primary domain's reputation. [L0307.025]
- If a cold list has low engagement, ISPs flag the domain as spam. [L0307.025]

### Authenticate the sending domain before the first send

- A campaign that warns that its emails are not authenticated using DNS records must be fixed before sending. Authentication through DNS records is required for providers such as Gmail or Outlook to show a sender identity. [L0864.018]
- The three authentication methods are SPF, DKIM and DMARC. SPF checks whether the sending server is on the domain's list of approved senders and flags the email if it is not. DKIM checks that the email has not been altered in transit. The sources name DMARC but do not explain how to set it up. [L0864.019]
- An SPF TXT record and a DKIM TXT record let receiving servers verify that mail from your domain is legitimate, which raises deliverability. *(Older: Source B, 2021-2022.)* [L0574.009]

#### Google Workspace SPF and DKIM setup (older: Source B, 2021-2022)

Before you start, check that the setup is ready:

1. In the Google Admin console, go to Apps > G Suite > Settings for Gmail and open Authenticate email. Before this, check under Apps > G Suite that the Gmail service shows ON for everyone in the organisation. [L0574.030]
2. The Authenticate email section may not appear until 24 hours after a new G Suite sign-up. Do not panic if it is missing. [L0574.008]

Then add the records:

1. Open the DNS settings of your domain at your domain host. The layout differs by provider, but the method is the same. [L0574.005]
2. Copy the existing TXT record that starts with `v=spf1 include:_spf.google.com`. G Suite adds this record automatically when it is connected to the domain. [L0574.005]
3. Add a new TXT record with host `SPF` in uppercase and paste the copied value as the TXT value. [L0574.005]
4. Click Save. [L0574.005]
5. In the Google Admin console, go to Apps > G Suite > Settings for Gmail and open Authenticate email. [L0574.005]
6. Select your sending domain and set the DKIM key bit length to 1024. [L0574.005, L0574.006]
7. Click Generate to create the new DKIM TXT record. [L0574.005]
8. Back in the DNS settings, add a new TXT record with host `google._domainkey` and paste the whole generated TXT value string into the TXT value field. Click Save. [L0574.005]
9. Return to Authenticate email and click Start authentication. Leave it running while DNS updates. [L0574.005]

Rules for the setup:

- **DKIM key length:** The lesson sets the key bit length to 1024 and stresses changing it from the default. The screen showed 2048 as the default. Do not keep that default when following this setup. [L0574.006]
- **DKIM record format:** The DKIM record host is `google._domainkey`, and the value begins with `v=DKIM1; k=rsa; p=`. [L0574.006]
- **Do not regenerate during authentication:** Generating a new DKIM TXT record stops email authentication until you click Start authentication again and DNS has updated. Do not regenerate the key while authentication is running unless you are prepared to restart it. [L0574.007]
- **Propagation time:** After Start authentication, DNS changes can take up to 48 hours to propagate. The status shows Authenticating email while it runs. [L0574.008]
- **One SPF record per domain:** A domain should publish only one SPF TXT record. If a record starting with `v=spf1` already exists, edit it instead of adding a second one. Two SPF records can make SPF checks fail. This check is an addition to the lesson's steps, not something it teaches. [L0574.010]
- **SPF qualifier:** The Google SPF value is `v=spf1 include:_spf.google.com` followed by a qualifier. The screens show `~all` (soft fail) before the change and `-all` (hard fail) after it. Choose the qualifier your policy needs and keep only one SPF record per domain. [L0574.029]
- **Keep the verification record:** Keep the existing `google-site-verification` TXT record in the DNS zone. It proves domain ownership to Google, and removing it can break the G Suite domain verification. [L0574.028]
- **Keep the mail routing records:** Leave the MX records in place when adding the SPF and DKIM TXT records. They route incoming mail to Google. [L0574.027]

Google Workspace mail routing (MX) records, as shown in the DNS panel of a G Suite domain at GoDaddy in 2021-2022 (older):

| Host | Priority |
|---|---|
| aspmx.l.google.com | 1 |
| alt1.aspmx.l.google.com | 5 |
| alt2.aspmx.l.google.com | 5 |
| aspmx2.googlemail.com | 10 |
| aspmx3.googlemail.com | 10 |

[L0574.027]

### Keep the domain's history clean

- If a domain is blacklisted before use, check its history with the Wayback Machine and by searching the domain name for reviews before buying a premium domain. [L0665.084]

### Understand sender reputation

- Sender reputation scores are bad, low, medium and high. Aim for medium or high at all times, because low and bad scores send most mail to spam. [L0633.005]
- A new provider does not assign a starting grade such as bad. The score is empty at first and needs a few weeks of sending to populate. The goal of the warm-up is to reach a positive reputation that you can carry forward before opening larger segments and volume. [L0633.006]
- Switching to a new email service provider resets your sender reputation to zero, even if you have mailed the same list for years. The reset applies at domain level, so the old reputation does not carry over to the new platform. [L0633.001]
- A newly registered sending domain also starts with no reputation, even when it uses the same IP addresses and infrastructure as before. A new domain needs its own warm-up. [L0633.002]
- The reset happens because the new provider sends through different servers and IP addresses. Mailbox providers have never seen this combination of domain, IP and infrastructure. They cannot use past performance, so the sender must build a new history through positive signals such as opens, clicks and replies. [L0633.003]
- Most email platforms with shared IPs, such as Klaviyo or ActiveCampaign, already manage warmed IP addresses, so IP warming is normally not needed. Only a business that manages its own dedicated IP or server needs a separate IP warm-up strategy, which should follow the provider's advice closely. [L0633.004]
- Each email provider publishes its own onboarding guidance for new domains, such as Klaviyo, Mailchimp or SendGrid. Follow that guidance, verify the domain and set up authentication protocols, because they differ by provider. [L0633.025]
- Google shows a domain reputation level, such as High or Medium, for each sending domain. During the warm-up, check this level alongside the email provider's own numbers, since the provider's metrics are not always fully accurate. [L0633.026]
- Continuously follow deliverability best practices after the warm-up. Keep cleaning the list, avoid spammy or misleading content, respect subscriber preferences, and make unsubscribing easy. These habits keep the sender reputation strong. [L0633.023]

### Prepare the list before a provider switch

Procedure before closing the old email provider:

1. Record the deliverability baseline for 30, 60 and 90 days: open rate, click rate, bounce rate, unsubscribe rate and spam complaints. [L0633.008]
2. Clean the list if it has not been cleaned in the last six months. Run all lists through a third-party email validator. [L0633.008]
3. Remove invalid addresses and addresses that cannot be reached, including hard bounces, before importing. [L0633.008]
4. Export the engagement data from the old provider: segments, suppression list and performance history. [L0633.008]
5. Tag every subscriber who opened or clicked in the last 90 days as engaged. Record behaviour (opens, clicks) and product interests where available. [L0633.008]
6. Import the list together with the tags so the new provider can separate engaged from unengaged subscribers. [L0633.008]
7. Set up email authentication (SPF, DKIM and DMARC) for the new provider, following its setup guide. [L0633.008]

Reasons behind the steps:

- Clean the list before import. Email addresses go invalid, mailboxes fill up, and Microsoft and Google delete inactive mailboxes. Bounces to such addresses count against your reputation, even if the list was double opt-in or verified at signup. [L0633.009]
- Old, unverified addresses cause bounces, which count negatively against your reputation. Mailboxes that Microsoft or Google have deleted for inactivity will bounce as well. [L0633.020]
- Migrating segments and engagement history matters because a new provider knows nothing about your subscribers beyond what you import. Without tags, the new list is one undifferentiated block and you cannot target the engaged group first. [L0633.011]
- Failing to migrate engagement data forces a random first send. This lowers opens and clicks and weakens the new reputation. Without history, the provider cannot separate engaged from unengaged subscribers. [L0633.019]
- Engaged subscribers are those who opened or clicked within the last 90 days. They should receive the first campaign on the new platform. Targeting the most engaged group gives the highest open and click rates, which rebuilds trust fastest. [L0633.010]

### Warm up a new domain or provider

Procedure after switching providers or registering a new domain:

1. Ask the new provider for its onboarding or warm-up guidance. For a very large list, request a custom warm-up plan that states the daily send volumes. [L0633.012]
2. On day one, send only to the most engaged segment so the first send stays under the provider's limit. Klaviyo's first send is below 3,000 recipients. Provider limits are usually below 10,000. [L0633.012]
3. Increase the volume each day by the percentage the provider allows. This is typically 10 to 15 percent and sometimes up to 50 percent. Never increase faster than that. [L0633.012]
4. Optionally connect an automated warm-up tool such as Warmup Inbox to the domain and let it run for a couple of months. [L0633.012]
5. Monitor open rate, click rate and inbox placement every day. If they drop or red flags appear, reduce the volume and review the content. [L0633.012]
6. After about three to four weeks of gradual increase, move to your usual email strategy and send to the full list. [L0633.012]

Volume and timing rules:

- **Daily increase, two sources:** The usual safe increase cited is 10 to 15 percent per day, occasionally up to 50 percent. Some providers allow the daily send volume to roughly double each day. Follow the exact criteria your provider gives you, because they reflect what is safe for that platform. [L0633.012, L0633.013]
- **Full warm-up duration:** The full warm-up period for a switch or a new domain takes about three to four weeks. Do not move to the full list before that period ends. [L0633.014]
- **Gradual plan:** A gradual volume plan over three to four weeks protects the new provider's reputation and avoids underperformance. A business that skips the plan and sends to a 100,000-subscriber list straight away is likely to run into trouble. [L0633.024]
- **Full list on day one:** Sending the full list on the first day after a switch, especially when open rates are already weak, leads to deliverability problems. Skipping the gradual ramp also makes the damage to reputation longer and harder to repair later. [L0633.018]
- **Non-linear curve:** The warm-up is not linear, so every business may need to adjust its schedule. Monitor it week by week and expect changes depending on list and content. [L0633.022]
- **Red flags:** Watch for falling open rates or click rates. If they drop, reduce the volume, check content for spammy or misleading elements, and then increase again more slowly. [L0633.021]
- **Recovery after a switch:** After a provider switch, open rates should recover gradually toward the levels recorded in the old provider's 30-, 60- and 90-day baseline. This recovery depends on the domain building positive interactions, which lets more mail reach the inbox instead of spam. [L0633.027]

### Control send timing and batching

- Campaign send mode: use Gradual send over several hours, starting at 11 am Eastern, with 20 percent of recipients per hour, instead of sending the whole list at once. [L0607.047]
- Sending a campaign to the whole list at one moment can overload the mail server and reduce deliverability. Spreading the send over several hours avoids this. [L0607.048]
- Build sends in engagement buckets (for example 0-14, 15-30, 31-60 and 31-90 days engaged) and send them in batches. Smaller batches make it easier to see where opens begin to fall. [L0864.021]

### Write content for the primary inbox

- Email deliverability depends heavily on content quality. If emails are landing in spam, control content quality first. [L0475.001]
- Text-based emails with personalization generally land in the primary inbox more often than image-heavy emails. Image-heavy emails can go to the Promotions tab and be left unopened. Brand logo, footer and a few supporting images can still be added on top of text-heavy designs. [L0573.017]
- Send more personalized emails that look less like ads, and include offers people want to open. Watch engagement, because opens signal to the inbox provider that subscribers want the mail. [L0510.049]
- A high click-through rate signals relevance to inbox providers such as Gmail. This supports deliverability and keeps emails in the primary inbox instead of the Promotions tab or spam folder. Opens without clicks over time signal that the email delivers little value. *(Older: Source B, 2021-2022.)* [L0602.006]
- Spam includes buying an email list, or emailing addresses that never opted in, such as contacts from an old address book. *(Older: Source A, 2022.)* [L0665.083]
- Newsletters must include a working unsubscribe link. Missing or broken unsubscribe options and re-subscribing people by other means are the main causes of blacklisting. *(Older: Source A, 2022.)* [L0665.083]

### Troubleshoot spam placement

Procedure if confirmation or order emails land in spam *(older: Source A, 2021)*:

1. Remove images from the affected emails and test again. [L0247.073]
2. Remove one trigger word at a time, such as "free", and send a test to a new address that is not in Klaviyo. [L0247.073]
3. Ask customers in early welcome emails to add the sender to their primary inbox. [L0247.073]
4. Ask early emails to elicit a reply with a specific question. [L0247.073]
5. Add a reply request to order confirmation emails to build engagement. [L0247.073]

Related rules:

- Sending to many bouncing addresses makes email providers such as Gmail treat the sender as a spammer. That lowers deliverability, so more emails end up in spam folders. *(Older: Source B, 2021-2022.)* [L0946.028]

### Understand Gmail tabs

- Emails often land in Gmail's Promotions tab or in spam. General tips are to send more personalized emails that look less like ads, include offers people want to open, and watch engagement. [L0510.049]
- Image-heavy emails can go to the Promotions tab and be left unopened. [L0573.017]
- A high click-through rate keeps emails in the primary inbox instead of the Promotions tab or spam folder. [L0602.006]

### Measure opens and clicks with Apple MPP

- Apple Mail Privacy Protection (MPP) opens emails automatically through a proxy to prevent user tracking. This breaks open tracking. [L0264.003]
- Track both opens and clicks instead. Opens measure non-MPP engagement. Clicks prove real user action and bypass the proxy for MPP users. [L0264.003]

### Benchmarks for open rates

The sources give no general open-rate benchmark. The figures below are examples from single lessons, not targets. [L0864.004]

| Figure | Meaning | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Gmail 21% open rate, Yahoo 11.5% | In one example, the lesson treats Yahoo as clearly struggling. Any provider far below the others should be isolated and worked on separately. | [L0864.004] |
| Hotmail open rate around 4% | A warning sign. Unsubscribes after a large send may come mostly from that provider, so check the unsubscribe rate per provider. | [L0864.013] |
| Provider open rate above 30% | The threshold for widening a temporary exclusion segment gradually. | [L0864.008] |

### Diagnose a provider whose deliverability is failing

Procedure:

1. Open the campaign's Deliverability tab and check Performance by inbox provider. This shows whether the weak engagement is limited to one provider. [L0864.008]
2. Open Performance by domain to pinpoint the provider or domain with the low open rate. [L0864.008]
3. Create an exclusion segment for that provider's unengaged profiles (no opens or clicks between 15 and 365 days ago). [L0864.008]
4. Add this segment to the Don't send to field of the next campaigns. Clone a campaign to reuse the recipient settings if needed. [L0864.008]
5. Keep the segment excluded for about 7 days. Then check the provider's open rate again in the Deliverability tab after each send. [L0864.008]
6. When the provider's open rate rises above 30%, widen the segment gradually by editing its definition (for example, changing the 15-day window to 25 days). Check again after a few days rather than changing it immediately. [L0864.008]

Principles:

- Excluding inactive profiles from a provider for a short period lets their sender reputation recover. This improves inbox placement for the active profiles. Excluding them is a temporary fix, so the exclusion has to be reviewed and loosened as metrics improve. [L0864.009]

### Recover from a blacklisting

Procedure if a domain is blacklisted for email *(older: Source A, 2022)*:

1. Identify which blacklist is listing it. [L0665.085]
2. Search for how to remove it from that specific provider. [L0665.085]
3. Follow their free delisting process. [L0665.085]

### Run the deliverability audit

- The deliverability audit needs the brand's domain with DNS access and test accounts at Gmail, Hotmail and Yahoo. [L1361.024]
- The /email-domain-audit skill then produces a deliverability health report with recommended fixes. [L1361.024]

## List Building and Opt-in Capture

### List Building and Opt-in Capture

### Why the list matters

- Around 80 percent of store visitors leave without buying and never return, so collecting their email address or phone number is needed to retarget them later. Email is the main way to turn these visitors into paying customers. [L0573.001]
- Roughly 80 to 90 percent of website visitors leave without buying, so a sign-up form that captures email addresses is the main way to recover them later. [L0607.004]
- A list built from visitors and offers gives you a traffic source that does not depend only on ads or organic social posts. [L0476.002]
- A list of subscribers can be sold to repeatedly over time, so lead ads should first collect the opt-in, and direct sales on the first click matter less. [L0641.020]
- Build an email list from pre-purchase visitors and keep first-time buyers engaged with email so they return for a second and third purchase. [L0609.006]
- Use the email list you collect from visitors for follow-up, because a visitor who does not buy on the first visit may still buy later. [L1002.073]
- Capture the visitor's email address while they are on the store and send follow-up emails to encourage them to complete the purchase. This recovers buyers who did not purchase during their first visit. [L0771.042]
- Use email campaigns for visitors who did not convert, because the list is an extra channel to bring back warm traffic after a cold ad. [L1016.069]
- A large promotion such as a Black Friday sale brings the most cash once a business has an established email list. That list should be built from abandoned carts, exit-intent overlay sign-ups and remarketing audiences. [L1446.010]
- An incentive that gets visitors to opt in to email is tied to a working opt-in and email program, which the lesson links to a 10% boost in customer value. [L1031.033]

### Prospect lists versus customer lists

- Prospect lists, made of visitors who have not bought yet, do not grow on their own the way customer lists do. They need deliberate opt-in offers and several list-building methods running at the same time. They take time to grow, so an early start and persistence matter. *Older advice (2021-22).* [L0571.001]
- Customer lists build by themselves through purchases, so the list-building methods below target non-buyers only. *Older advice (2021-22).* [L0571.005]
- Seven proven methods build a prospect list: content opt-ins, discount offers, quizzes, surveys, contests and giveaways, sales event opt-ins, and lead magnets as the content-based variant. Content opt-ins are the main workhorse. *Older advice (2021-22).* [L0571.005]
- Content opt-ins work best for visitors at the top and middle of the funnel who are researching and need education to solve a problem. For visitors near the point of purchase they are often unattractive, because they need less education. *Older advice (2021-22).* [L0571.006]
- Interest-based newsletters on top-of-funnel articles are a useful capture. Subscribers can receive discounts or new-launch messages later, once trust is built, which turns anonymous readers into future buyers. [L1269.032]
- Capture email addresses in as many places on the site as possible, because these emails are needed for remarketing to shoppers who do not buy on the first visit. *Older advice (2019).* [L1275.069]
- Use email capture at multiple points, such as the footer and an exit-intent overlay, to build the list that later drives revenue. *Older advice (2022).* [L0854.082]
- Use the collected emails for remarketing and not just as a newsletter list. *Older advice (2018).* [L1085.003]
- Collect emails with exit-intent overlays and at purchase. Use the list to make offers and to remarket to past buyers and opt-in visitors. *Older advice (2019).* [L0663.036]

### Choosing the offer

#### Match the offer to the goal

- Match the opt-in offer to what subscribers want in the long term, not only to a first-order discount. A 5 or 10 percent coupon can work in the short term, but an offer tied to the customer's real goal keeps subscribers closer to the brand over time. [L0380.007]
- A bribe only earns an email address when it delivers something the visitor already wants. Build it from the site's topic and the visitor's likely stage, not from what the affiliate would like to promote. [L0514.038]
- Offer value that moves the customer toward the goal behind the product, not only the product itself. A podcast microphone buyer wants a successful show, so guides on launching and growing a podcast add value. The list works as a two-way exchange: the reader gets what they want, and you get their attention and eventually their purchase. [L0380.009]
- Asking visitors openly for their email in an opt-in pop-up is appropriate when the offer is genuinely useful and matches what the subscriber will receive. Visitors who want the content expect the request, so hesitating to ask weakens the list-building step. The promise in the pop-up must be exactly what the list delivers. [L0380.035]
- Use a community or cause in the offer. An offer that pairs a small incentive with a cause the reader already cares about gives a reason to subscribe that fits the article's topic. [L1269.045]

#### Incentive types

- Common incentive opt-in offers on Shopify stores include 10% off the next order, $10 off the first order, and a spin-the-wheel pop-up that gives a random discount in exchange for an email address. A concrete discount makes the sign-up feel worth it, while a generic newsletter invitation gives the visitor no clear reward. [L0510.062]
- A sign-up offer that gives a discount in exchange for an email address works because a clear, concrete reward makes the sign-up feel worth it. Two examples in the same pattern: `Subscribe now and get 15% off your first purchase.` and `Join the list for free shipping on your next order.` [L0510.047]
- The sign-up incentive is a discount, typically 5 percent, 5 dollars or 10 percent off, offered in exchange for the email address. [L0573.006]
- Offer an incentive such as 10% to 20% off in exchange for signing up on a signup form page. [L1213.055]
- Offer free shipping at the moment a visitor arrives to exchange their email address. This captured emails at a rate of about 20% in the example. [L0790.069]
- Where a discount does not fit the business, a buyer's guide or product quiz can replace it as the reason to sign up, followed by an automated sequence. [L1204.020]
- Offer a valuable lead magnet, such as a buyer's guide or a quiz with results that unlock after entering an email, to collect emails when a discount alone does not work. *Older advice (2022).* [L1113.067]
- Three list-building offer types for campaigns: [L0307.030]
  1. Membership or subscription: exclusive member-only deals, limited spots (for example 100), free to join.
  2. Contest or giveaway: enter to win a high-value prize (for example a $1,000 gift card), subscribe to enter.
  3. Lead magnet (evergreen): a valuable free resource (PDF, guide, template, checklist) usually priced at $19 to $29, available free for a limited time or limited quantity (for example 100 copies).
- Pattern: replace the default footer sign-up text with a prize-entry line that names a monthly giveaway, because people sign up for a chance to win more readily than for updates. Two examples: `Subscribe for your chance to win a $100 gift card this month` and `Join the list to enter this month's giveaway of a free starter kit`. [L1204.019]

#### Discount codes and their rules

Sources give different minimum-spend rules. Both are kept; each applies to the setup it describes.

| Source setup | Discount | Minimum spend | Usage limit | Ids |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Shopify, Amount off order, generic sign-up | 5%, $5 or 10% off | None | One use per customer | L0573.006 |
| Welcome code example `welcome_10` | 10% off | None | One use per customer | L0573.007 |
| Shopify fixed-amount code for a sign-up pop-up, example `save five` | $5 off | Optional, example $50 | Once per customer if desired | L0477.016 |

- Create the discount in Shopify under Discounts > Create discount > Amount off order. [L0573.006]
- A 10 percent welcome code named `welcome_10` with no minimum order value and one use per customer is used as the sign-up discount. Set the coupon type in the form to static coupon when one code is shared by all subscribers. [L0573.007]
- For a fixed-amount sign-up discount, follow these steps. [L0477.016]
  1. In Shopify, create a discount with a fixed amount ($5 in the example).
  2. Give it a memorable name (for example `save five`).
  3. Limit usage to once per customer if desired.
  4. Add an optional minimum purchase (for example $50).
  5. Put the exact code into the Klaviyo success step.
- A sign-up form can issue a unique discount code to each subscriber instead of one shared static code. Unique codes let you track redemptions per person. A static code is simpler to set up and is the usual starting choice for a new store. [L0573.032]
- Replace the offer text with a fixed dollar amount, for example `save $5 with your first order`. [L1297.016]

### Lead magnets, buyer's guides, quizzes and giveaways

#### Lead magnet design

- A lead magnet should give real value to the reader. A useful resource that people want to keep works better over the long term than a single discount for a referring site. It should also resemble a marketing brochure, with the company introduced and an offer included. [L1076.014]
- About 30 to 40 percent of people who opt in open the email and click the download link for a lead magnet. Make the content worth reading for that group, while people who do not read it still receive the promotional emails. [L1076.016]
- A free downloadable buyer's guide for the product category can serve as a lead magnet, exchanged for an email sign-up. Adding a coupon after sign-up gives the prospect a second reason to convert and builds trust before Shopping ads are shown. [L1142.013]
- A free buyer's guide for a niche works well as a content asset, because it captures emails that can be used later in promotions. *Older advice (2022).* [L1092.097]
- Content pieces should include an opt-in form to collect email leads, even when the page also links out to third-party sites. *Older advice (2021-22).* [L1421.029]
- Every blog post should offer readers a chance to join the email list, for example through a free download in exchange for their email address. *Older advice (2021-22).* [L0462.021]

#### Buyer's guide procedures

Procedure A: lead magnet with a Klaviyo flow. *Older advice (2022).* [L1092.095]
1. Create a buyer's guide PDF for the niche.
2. Build an opt-in page on Shopify with an embedded Klaviyo sign-up box that asks for the email address and promises to send the guide there.
3. Set the sign-up form to add the subscriber to a list named for the guide, such as buyer's guide, or to apply a tag.
4. Create a Klaviyo flow triggered when the tag is applied or the subscriber joins the list.
5. Add a short wait of 5 minutes, then send the email containing the PDF.

Procedure B: Shopify page or exit-intent overlay. *Older advice in the source year range (2023 source).* [L1094.102]
1. Create a Shopify page titled `Buyer's Guide` with an opt-in form asking for an email address.
2. Or set up an exit-intent overlay that appears as visitors leave and offers the guide in exchange for an email address.
3. Connect the form to a Shopify email automation that sends the PDF guide to the subscriber.

Procedure C: buyer's guide on a dedicated page. *Older advice (2022).* [L1281.047]
1. Create a content page on the store that describes the buyer's guide, not a product.
2. Embed the Klaviyo opt-in form on that page.
3. Send the guide by email through Klaviyo when the visitor submits the form. You can also use the exit-intent overlay alongside it.

Exit-intent text for the buyer's guide, ready to use. Keep the email field as the only input, and send the guide automatically after submission. *Older advice (2022).* [L1281.048]

```
Before you go, enter your email and we will send you our free buyer's guide.
```

#### Quizzes, surveys and contests

- Offer a quiz with results that unlock after entering an email. [L1113.067]
- In a contest or giveaway, extra entries can be earned through actions such as social sharing and referrals, which increases the number of opt-ins. Promote the contest on social media to grow both the entries and the reach. *Older advice (2021-22).* [L0571.032]
- Instagram giveaways can collect email addresses for a new brand's list: followers enter the draw by submitting their email. This suits a business that still needs to grow its audience before it has a large list. *Older advice (2021-22).* [L0462.042]
- Contest, giveaway and membership offers are set out in the three-offer structure above. [L0307.030]

#### Sales event opt-ins

- Opt-in form for a sales event: one field for an email address, a "Sign Up" button, and directly beneath the form this note. *Older advice (2021-22).* [L0571.033]

```
*Submitting this form means you agree to get our emails, and you may opt out whenever you like.
```

- A large promotion such as a Black Friday sale brings the most cash once a business has an established email list. [L1446.010]

#### Gamified capture

- Besides standard sign-up boxes in the sidebar, footer and abandoned-cart emails, lead capture can use exit-intent overlays and gamified spin-to-win pop-ups such as Wheelio that offer a prize in exchange for an email address. These tools collect leads from visitors who already come to the site, so they add to the reach of paid traffic instead of replacing it. [L1076.074]

### Where to place capture points

#### Placement principles

- Install an opt-in on the store before publishing content, because articles alone do not build an email list. [L0380.006]
- The email sign-up should be on the store even before you plan email campaigns, because it collects subscribers into the Shopify admin from day one. Keep the heading short and clear about what visitors get. [L0775.054]
- Each email opt-in on the site should have its own email list. Specific opt-ins can be placed on relevant content pages, and exit-intent pop-ups should be reviewed as an opt-in opportunity. *Older advice (2021-22).* [L0493.009]
- Map every page of the site and note which opt-ins already run there. [L0571.027]
  1. Map every page of the site (home page, collection pages, blog page, blog posts, product pages) and note which opt-ins already run there.
  2. Identify the pages without any opt-in opportunity.
  3. Decide which opt-in to place on each gap, matched to the visitor's funnel stage.
- Content opt-ins need their own list or segment, with an automated email sequence behind them. The sequence should move the subscriber from the content toward a purchase, for example by leading a how-to downloader toward the relevant product. *Older advice (2021-22).* [L0571.008]
- A newsletter works as an opt-in when it is specific about its content and frequency, for example one issue each month with a stated topic. Offering an immediate download or a sample of the newsletter on the page lets visitors judge its value before they opt in. *Older advice (2021-22).* [L0571.012]

#### Footer

- Add an email opt-in to the footer for the mailing list. Niche theme stores rely heavily on email to follow up, run holiday promotions and win repeat sales. [L1325.023]
- In the theme's Newsletter section, set the heading, subheading, email placeholder text and button label, and include a short promise such as `stay updated with offers` so visitors know what they sign up for. [L1188.041]
- On a simple product store, place an email sign-up box in the footer with the heading `Sign Up And Save` and the line `Get early access to new product releases and special discounts.` Add a `40% OFF` sale banner with `Sale Ends SOON` next to it to create urgency, and ask visitors where they are based in the sign-up form. [L0347.039]

#### Exit intent

- An exit-intent overlay that collects emails from visitors who are about to leave is the main source of email leads for a store. It builds a list from real visitors who opted in. *Older advice (2024 source, same principle repeated across sources).* [L0471.023]
- The exit-intent overlay produced most of the collected emails in the source example. [L1204.020]
