- Exit-intent overlays on the store help gather new email subscribers organically. This is how a list grows after launch, alongside abandoned cart contacts. [L1285.063]
- Exit-intent overlays on the store capture visitors' email addresses when they try to leave. Together with the abandoned cart flow, they build the email list organically after launch, even if the store is new before Q4. [L1223.019]
- Use an exit-intent overlay that captures emails and starts an automated follow-up sequence. Pair it with abandoned cart emails. *Older advice (2020).* [L1088.086]
- If it is unclear whether a pop-up comes from Shopify or Klaviyo, check Klaviyo first, because a Klaviyo pop-up is visible there. [L0855.118]

#### Blog and content pages

- After an email signup section is added to the Manhattan blog post template and saved, the opt-in appears at the bottom of every blog post, below the content. It is visible on the live store after a page refresh. [L1265.014]
- Build an opt-in form on the content page, not only on product pages, to collect leads from research visitors. [L0571.027]

#### Dedicated sign-up page

- Build an email list with a sign-up form page on the Shopify store that asks for email, name and optionally phone number, instead of a buy-now product page. Offer an incentive such as 10% to 20% off in exchange for signing up. [L1213.055]
- Lead generation traffic should be sent to a page whose only goal is to collect name and email, not to sell a product. Ads that promote a discount or a best-seller page skip the list-building step and lose the buyer who is not ready yet. [L1076.005]

### Pop-up set-up

#### Pop-up timing and triggers

Sources use different triggers, each tied to the tool they describe. Use the setting that matches the platform you are configuring.

| Tool or platform | Trigger | Ids |
|---|---|---|
| Clavio pop-up | Exit intent, plus a 10-second delay after page load | L0476.006, L0634.002 |
| Klaviyo pop-up (Source C) | Show after 10 seconds, plus a scroll rule at 30 to 50 percent of the page, with the match set to show only when all selected rules are met | L0573.008 |
| Shopify theme pop-up | Delay timer, for example after 60 seconds of visitor inactivity | L0973.005 |

- A pop-up that appears immediately on landing is considered too aggressive and annoys visitors, so a 10-second delay is preferred. Adding a scroll trigger at 30 to 50 percent ensures the visitor has seen half the content and is more likely interested in the offer. [L0573.009]
- Delaying the pop-up and not showing it to repeat visitors reduces annoyance and keeps the opt-in rate stable. *Older advice (2022).* [L0803.088]
- The email pop-up in the theme settings asks visitors for their email address, which makes it a lead capture tool. The exit pop-up does not ask for an email address; it only shows text, an optional image and an optional discount code. [L0973.006]
- In the same theme section you can choose an image, attach a discount code and change all of the pop-up text. [L0973.005]

#### Clavio pop-up procedure

Clavio sign-up form, covering the set-up described in the sources. [L0476.006]
1. Open Sign-up forms in Clavio and pick a template that works on both desktop and mobile.
2. Keep or rename the form (example name: `Subscribe for coupon`) and choose the list (example: newsletter).
3. Click Create form.
4. Replace the image with your logo, change the headline and the button text so they state the discount, and add your own text where needed.
5. Open Styles and choose Pop-up as the display type, then set the background colour to match your store. Optional: add a background image.
6. Open Targeting and behaviour, choose Show based on rules, and set exit intent plus a 10-second delay after page load.
7. Open the success screen, enter the coupon code you created in Shopify (example: `save five`), and adjust the text and text colour.
8. Click the top-right Publish, then Publish anyway. The pop-up is now live.

- The same Clavio set-up in brief: open Sign up forms, choose a template that works on desktop and mobile, keep the list as Newsletter, set the form Styles to Pop-up, and under Targeting and behaviour use Show based on rules with an exit-intent trigger and a 10-second delay after page load. [L0634.002, L0476.006]

#### Klaviyo sign-up form

- Klaviyo sign-up form creation: Create > Email only > select the Newsletter list > Next > choose Pop-up > Next. Do not include a teaser, since teasers can annoy mobile visitors. Add one only if the sign-up rate drops. [L0573.005]
- Pop-ups are reported to have the highest conversion among Klaviyo sign-up form types, compared with full-page and fly-out forms. [L0573.004]
- Set the form status from Draft to Live, then check the layout in the mobile preview before publishing. [L0573.011]
- Test the layout in the mobile preview, since most customers arrive from phones. [L0573.008]
- Creating a Klaviyo sign-up pop-up with a discount: [L1297.016]
  1. Go to Signup forms in the left menu and pick a pop-up design.
  2. Name the form and add it to the newsletter subscriber list, then click Create form.
  3. Replace the offer text with a fixed dollar amount, for example `save $5 with your first order`.
  4. Replace the image with a product photo or a gift image from unsplash.com.
  5. Set the success step to show the discount code you created in Shopify.
  6. Click Publish, then Publish form.
- Klaviyo is an email marketing tool. Its embedded sign-up form on the store lets visitors enter name and email before they download a lead magnet. [L0649.050]
- Klaviyo can collect names and email addresses from prospects on lead generation offer forms. [L0238.013]

#### Other tools

- SumoMe (sumo.com) is a free email opt-in pop-up tool that can be added to Shopify, WooCommerce and most other platforms. [L0380.006]
- From the first day of the store, collect visitor emails with a pop-up tool such as Privy so you can remarket to them later. *Older advice (2018).* [L1085.003]
- OptinMonster is an opt-in tool that can record which page or content piece a subscriber joined from. This makes it simple to place each subscriber into the matching list. *Older advice (2021-22).* [L0603.019]
- Optimunk's Email input goes to Klaviyo's email property, and its Phone input goes to phone_number. The SMS Club Invite input has no fixed Klaviyo target: you either choose a value from the list or type one in. [L1138.011]
- Under Custom value, send extra data with each sign-up: Campaign name, URL of campaign (the page where the pop-up appeared) and Variant name. Add more custom values if needed. The URL of campaign is set as the Root URL. [L1138.012]
- Sending the URL of the page where the pop-up appeared lets follow-up emails return the visitor to that exact page. This is better than sending them to the homepage or a collection page, which forces them to find the product again and adds friction. [L1138.013]

#### Shopify sign-up section

- Email signup section settings include the colour scheme, heading and subheading text, and the form itself, which cannot be restyled. Emails entered here are stored as subscribers in the Shopify admin. [L0775.053]
- Email signup section: colour scheme set to accent 2. The heading and short text can be edited, but the email form itself cannot be customised. Addresses entered there are stored as subscribers in the Shopify admin. [L1309.055]

#### Sign-up pop-up for a discount: copy and layout

- Replace the copy with wording that fits the niche and product, and change the image to one related to the niche or a gifting image. [L0573.010]

### Pop-up and opt-in copy

#### Headline formulas

- Opt-in pop-up headline formula: name a concrete result the reader wants, then ask for the email directly below in the same box with a clear call to action. It works because it sells the outcome instead of a discount, and asking for the email feels like a useful exchange. New examples: `See the exact five-step plan I used to cut product return rates in half. Enter your email below to get it.` Or: `Learn the three tools that cut my ad testing time by a third. Drop your email to receive the list.` [L0380.008]
- Email signup headline pattern: name the community or group the subscriber joins plus a fast benefit, such as inviting them to be first to see new arrivals. Example 1: `Join the Crew and get first access to restocks.` Example 2: `Join the Club for early sale alerts.` [L1309.056]
- Pattern: replace the generic newsletter line (for example a plain `subscribe to our newsletter`) with a short invitation that names the brand and its community or benefit. The brand name makes the line feel personal and the community word makes joining feel like belonging. Examples: `Join the Harbor Crew` and `Get early access to [Brand] drops`. [L1018.015]
- An email capture offer works when it pairs a small incentive with a cause the reader already cares about. Two examples in this pattern: `Get 15% off your first order and help fund a reef restoration project` and `Join our weekly rewilding newsletter and receive 12% off when you sign up for our forest-friendly range`. [L1269.045]

#### Weak and strong opt-in copy

- Weak opt-in copy: `Join our email list today to receive our newsletter and discounts.` Strong opt-in copy: `Join our email list today and we'll send you our 50 page guide to sending emails that convert.` The strong version names one concrete asset that solves a specific problem. *Older advice (2021-22).* [L0571.004]

#### Standard pop-up copy (sign-up discount)

Copy for a 10 percent sign-up discount, as a starting point to adapt to your brand. [L0573.010]

```
Headline: "Want to get 10% off your first purchase?"
Sub-line: "Sign up to unlock your discount."
Email field placeholder: "Add your best email"
Button: "Reveal my discount"
Decline link: "No thanks, I'd rather pay full price"
After submitting, the success message should say "Thanks for signing up. Here is your discount code" followed by the code, with the confirmation line removed.
```

#### Exit-intent and pre-exit copy

Pre-exit copy, verbatim, for an exit pop-up that captures an email and unlocks a discount. [L0154.002]

```
Pause before leaving: share your email address to unlock a special promotional discount from [STORE.COM].
```

#### Welcome copy

Welcome message that confirms the discount code is on its way. [L0418.035]

```
Welcome to [Brand Name]. You're in. Your [10]% discount code is on its way to your inbox. If it hasn't arrived after a few minutes, check your spam or promotions folder, because our emails don't always land in the main inbox.
```

#### Pop-up principles

- Asking visitors openly for their email in an opt-in pop-up is appropriate when the offer is genuinely useful and matches what the subscriber will receive. [L0380.035]
- The email pop-up asks visitors for their email address. The exit pop-up does not. [L0973.006]
- Keep the heading short and clear about what visitors get. [L0775.054]

### Lead-gen ads and paid sources

- Lead ads should collect email addresses first through an opt-in offer, because a subscribed list can be sold to repeatedly over time. Direct sales on the first click are less important than building the list. Offers such as a free gift or discount coupon are used to earn the opt-in. [L0641.020]
- Use conversion campaigns with `lead` as the conversion event, not Facebook Lead Ads, because lead form quality is significantly lower than landing page conversions. Lead form integration via Zapier to Klaviyo adds friction and can cause data sync problems. [L0307.002]
- Lead generation traffic should be sent to a page whose only goal is to collect name and email, not to sell a product. [L1076.005]
- Use the lead-gen traffic pattern above before sending ads to a discount or best-seller page. [L1076.005]
- Procedure for collecting leads and sending remarketing. Collect email addresses on the store, and track visitors with pixels so they can be shown ads on Meta and Google that return them to the store. Use abandoned cart and monthly promotion emails for email follow-up. *Older advice (2024).* [L1115.080]

```
1. Collect email addresses on the store.
2. Track visitors with pixels so they can be shown ads on Meta and Google that return them to the store.
3. Use abandoned cart and monthly promotion emails for email follow-up.
```

- Ads that promote a discount or a best-seller page skip the list-building step, so route them to a collection page first. [L1076.005]
- Shopping ads are shown after a prospect has signed up for a buyer's guide and received a coupon. [L1142.013]

### Influencer and partner sources

- Structure influencer incentives (exclusive coupons, free products, free training) to drive email newsletter subscriptions, not just traffic. This extends the relationship beyond the influencer's first promotion and enables long-term marketing to the audience by email. [L0226.047]
- Partner with other companies that have a list and run a drop with them, offering their audience an exclusive offer to get started. The partner's audience must match your niche. *Older advice (2021-22).* [L0768.005]
- Ways to acquire first customers through email: *Older advice (2021-22).* [L0768.005]
  1. Email your existing customers.
  2. Partner with other companies that have a list and run a drop with them, offering their audience an exclusive offer to get started.
- Legitimate email list sources are existing customers, abandoned checkouts and website pop-ups. Before paying for more subscribers, measure how much revenue an exit-intent overlay opt-in brings in per sign-up, and scale acquisition only after an email campaign already converts well. *Older advice (2024 source).* [L0836.060]
- Import an email list from a previous employer only with permission and only if the contacts are relevant to the niche. Then import the list into Klaviyo and send a short introduction broadcast. *Older advice (2021).* [L0247.074]

### List structure, naming and sources

#### Naming

- Name lists to show their source clearly: `Optimunk 10% OFF`, `Giveaway XYZ`, `Checkout`, `Customer`. This makes acquisition history traceable when reviewing performance. [L0264.005]
- Each content opt-in needs its own list or segment, with its own automated email sequence behind it. [L0571.008]
- Each email opt-in on the site should have its own email list. [L0493.009]

#### Discount list flow

Procedure for a discount opt-in flow, in this order. *Older advice (2021-22).* [L0571.015]
1. Create a dedicated list for discount subscribers that feeds automatic discount reminder emails.
2. Send a few emails to encourage completion of the sale if the visitor does not buy immediately.
3. Add a rule that moves any subscriber who becomes a customer out of the discount list into the new customer list, so they stop receiving discount reminders.

#### Source tracking

- Record which page or content piece a subscriber joined from, so each subscriber lands in the matching list. [L0603.019]
- Send the page URL with each sign-up so follow-up can return the visitor to that page. [L1138.013]

### Consent settings

#### Opt-in process

- The opt-in process is set per list under Klaviyo list > Settings > Consent > Opt-in process. [L1138.008]
- Double opt-in is Klaviyo's recommendation. It requires confirmation and helps block invalid and malicious contacts. It needs the list's confirmation email and the global SMS confirmation text kept up to date. [L1138.008]
- Single opt-in adds subscribers immediately without confirmation. Klaviyo recommends regular list cleaning with it. [L1138.008]
- Changing the consent setting of a new list is easy to forget. Check the opt-in process on every new pop-up list before the pop-up goes live, because it determines whether subscribers must confirm. [L1138.009]
- The opt-in form note for a sales event tells visitors they agree to receive emails and may opt out at any time. [L0571.033]

#### Unsubscribe behaviour

- In Klaviyo, each list has an unsubscribe setting under Settings > Unsubscribes that decides whether an unsubscribe from that list also removes the person from all future emails. Check this option when you create a pop-up list, because the account may apply a default that treats any unsubscribe as a full email unsubscribe. Use the View settings link to review the account-level default before going live. [L1138.016]

#### Go-live gate

- Go live with the pop-up only after the Klaviyo welcome series is ready, so new subscribers receive their first sequence right away. If a welcome series already exists for the client, copy its structure and adapt it rather than building from scratch. [L1138.015]

### Never buy lists

- Do not buy email lists to acquire customers. *Older advice (2021-22).* [L0768.006]
- Buying or renting email lists is a serious mistake. Emailing people who never opted in causes spam complaints, which can get your domain blacklisted from major inboxes such as Google and Yahoo. [L0471.022]
- Only email people who opted into your store list. Purchased email lists cause spam complaints and damage your sending reputation, so the people you want to reach stop opening your emails. [L1285.062]
- Do not buy, rent or email purchased lists, or email people who do not know the brand. Spam complaints quickly get the sending domain blacklisted, and legitimate emails stop reaching inboxes. [L1116.036]
- Never send cold emails directly to purchased email lists. Instead: [L0307.013]
  1. Upload the CSV to Facebook or Google as a custom audience.
  2. Run ads sending them to your opt-in landing page.
  3. Let them re-opt-in.
  4. Then send emails to the engaged list.
  This prevents domain blacklisting. If you email a cold list with a low open rate, ISPs (Google, Hotmail, Outlook) interpret it as spam and blacklist your sender domain, blocking future emails. Example: sending to 1,000 with only 5 opens triggers ISP spam flags.
- When uploading existing email lists to email platforms like Klaviyo, recipients may need to re-opt-in depending on setup. Send in Blue allows bypassing this re-opt-in requirement if needed. [L0307.014]

### Measured expectations

| Measure | Value | When it applies | Ids |
|---|---|---|---|
| Visitors who leave without buying | Around 80% (general figure) | Typical store traffic | L0573.001 |
| Visitors who leave without buying | Roughly 80 to 90% | Alternative figure for website visitors | L0607.004 |
| Pop-up opt-in rate, one store | 0.6 to 1% | Reported from one store (2022, older) | L0803.088 |
| Pop-up opt-in rate, average | About 3 to 5% | Average reported across stores (2022, older) | L0803.088 |
| Target capture of cold-traffic visitors | 15% to 20% | Realistic goal for cold traffic | L0790.068 |
| Free shipping offered on arrival | About 20% captured | Example result | L0790.069 |
| Opt-ins who open and click the lead magnet download | 30% to 40% | Share of opt-ins | L1076.016 |

- A weekly target was 150,000 site visitors and at least 10,000 new emails. [L0790.068]
- The lesson links a working opt-in and email program to a 10% boost in customer value. [L1031.033]
- Before paying for more subscribers, measure revenue per sign-up from exit-intent opt-ins. [L0836.060]
- Early on, a list grows slowly, so expect a long build and keep at it. *Older advice (2021-22).* [L0571.001]

### Following up new sign-ups

#### Automated follow-up

- Use a welcome series for new subscribers, copied from an existing client series where one exists. [L1138.015]
- Follow-up sequence after a sign-up: send the welcome email and a discount reminder, then move the subscriber onward. [L0571.015]
- Use abandoned cart and monthly promotion emails for email follow-up. [L1115.080]

#### Daily follow-up schedule

Procedure for follow-up of new email sign-ups. *Older advice (2020).* [L0967.077]
1. Email new sign-ups once a day during the first few days.
2. After that, move them to the main broadcast list.
3. Send to the main broadcast list at least once per week, sometimes twice.

#### Manual outreach

Manual outreach to new email sign-ups in the early stage. *Older advice (2022).* [L1092.085]
1. Take the email address from an exit-intent or opt-in capture.
2. Send a short personal email from the owner that asks which products or questions the person has.
3. Answer every reply to earn trust and start conversations.

- Reply to new email leads personally, asking if they have questions and offering help. Manual outreach at the start builds trust and sales, and is especially useful before automation is in place. [L0972.080]
- Use this follow-up for a new email lead who signed up for your discount. [L0972.079]

```
Hi [customer name], this is [your name], the owner of [store name]. I noticed you signed up for our [discount code] offer, and I wanted to see if you have any questions. If there is anything I can help you with, just reply to this email. I'm happy to help.
```

- Use the email list you collect from visitors for follow-up, since a visitor who does not buy on the first visit may still buy later. *Older advice (2019).* [L1002.073]

#### Further details

- Collecting email signups before launch is not useful, because nobody finds the store before it goes live. Audiences built in ad platforms before launch do the job of building the pre-launch base. [L0934.059]

## Lists, Segments, Exclusions and List Hygiene

### List vs segment logic

- A list is a data source. It shows where a subscriber came from. [L0264.004]
- A segment is a rule-based profile. It defines who meets specific engagement criteria. [L0264.004]
- A list is a group of profiles that opted in, such as the Newsletter list. A segment is a group of profiles filtered by conditions, such as past orders or behavior. Both are found under Audience, then Lists and segments. [L0607.041]
- Klaviyo provides pre-built lists such as Newsletter. Custom segments are created with Create list or segment under Audience > Lists and segments. [L0573.030]
- Klaviyo can read order history from Shopify or another connected store platform. This allows automatic segmentation by number of purchases and by products or product types bought. Segments are used for customers, while separate lists based on opt-in source are often used for prospects. [L0603.018]
- *Older advice (2021):* In Klaviyo, the only list to create for this setup is the broadcast list. Default segments that appear in new accounts are not needed because automations do not use them. [L0469.102]
- The broadcast list contains every subscriber who has not opted out, whatever the source: abandoned cart, purchase, footer signup or exit-intent signup. These contacts receive all monthly promotions. [L1204.015]
- Split audiences into two kinds of lists:
  - Communication lists: email lists that receive your messages.
  - Invisible lists: used only for ad targeting and never receive email.
  
  Keeping these apart keeps targeting precise. [L0757.007]

### Engagement-recency segments

Build overlapping recency segments (7-day, 14-day, 0-30, 31-60, 61-90, 90+) so you can test audiences and rotate them across different engagement windows. [L0264.013]

| Segment | Condition | Use | IDs |
|---|---|---|---|
| 7-Day Hyper-Engaged | (Opened Email at least 3 times in last 7 days) OR (Clicked Email at least 2 times in last 7 days) | Rebuilding deliverability. Typical usage: 2 weeks. Send 3-7 emails within 7 days to this segment. | L0264.006 |
| 14-Day Super-Engaged | (Opened Email at least 3 times in last 14 days) OR (Clicked Email at least 3 times in last 14 days) | Deliverability rebuilding. If open rates reach 60-80%, continue for 3-4 sends, then expand to wider segments. | L0264.007 |
| 0-30 Days Engaged | (Opened Email at least 1 time in 0-30 days) OR (Clicked Email at least 1 time in 0-30 days) | Core segment for the regular sending strategy. | L0264.008 |
| 31-60 Days Engaged | (Opened Email at least 1 time in 31-60 days) OR (Clicked Email at least 1 time in 31-60 days) | Regular sending rotation. | L0264.009 |
| 61-90 Days and 90+ Days Engaged | Same threshold: at least 1 open or click in the respective window | Build matching segments for these windows. | L0264.009 |
| All Active | (Subscribed at least once over all time) OR (Received Email AND Can receive email marketing) | Includes all active subscribers except suppressed addresses. | L0264.010 |

- Target open rate is above 30%. When open rates fall below this, move to a more recent engagement window to rebuild deliverability. [L0264.002]
- Email sending works like driving: gradually expand your audience until open rates drop, then revert to a tighter engagement window. Find the sweet spot where volume and deliverability stay balanced by monitoring performance and adjusting audience size. [L0264.001]
- Continuing to email contacts who have not engaged is only justified while deliverability stays good. If deliverability weakens, stop mailing these contacts. [L0494.017]

### Behavior, purchase-count and VIP segments

- Segmentation divides the list into groups based on behavior, such as pages viewed, time spent on the site, purchase history, and how the person joined the list. Relevant messages to each group generally produce better results, so more segmentation usually means more valuable messages sent more often. *(Older advice, 2021-22.)* [L0603.015]
- Email segmentation uses the same logic as retargeting audiences: group people by their actions and interests so each group gets a message suited to where it is in the buying journey. *(Older advice, 2021-22.)* [L0603.016]
- Minimum segments to set up in the email software *(older advice, 2021-22)*: [L0603.017]
  1. Customer segments by purchase count: customers who bought once, customers who bought twice, and customers who bought three or more times.
  2. A segment of all customers who have bought.
  3. Product-based segments for best-selling products or products with a clear upsell or repurchase cycle, such as items that wear out or have a limited shelf life.
  4. Customer segments by product type if the store sells several product types.
  5. Prospect lists by opt-in source: one list per specific content opt-in, one per product page opt-in, and one per collection page or product type opt-in.
  6. Add new segments or lists each time a new opt-in is added to the website.
- Set up audience segments in Klaviyo: buyers, non-buyers and VIP customers, where VIP means a customer who has bought more than once. Exclude buyers from offer launches for products they already bought. [L0605.025]
- Example VIP segment: customers whose Placed order count is greater than one (two or more orders). Use it to target loyal buyers with product launches or early access. [L0573.031]
- VIP segment setup:
  1. Under Audience, then Lists and segments, click Create list or segment and create a segment.
  2. Set the condition to the Placed Order metric with a count greater than 1, meaning two or more orders.
  
  [L0607.042]
- A VIP customer is defined here as a customer with two or more orders (Placed Order count greater than 1). [L0607.043]
- Create Klaviyo segments for shoppers who bought Checkout Plus and for those who did not, then target them differently. The integration does not come out of the box, but it can be built. [L0955.046]
- After identifying high-performing engagement segments (30%+ open rates), layer on product-specific conditions to increase targeting precision: "viewed products with [attribute]", "looked at [collection]", or "purchased from [collection]". Example: add "AND viewed products containing protein" to the 0-30 Days Engaged segment to target engaged subscribers interested in protein. [L0264.015]
- Audience segmentation produces 8 to 10 segments deployed in Klaviyo, built from existing subscriber data without any extra tool. [L1361.025]
- Add a checklist of product interests and a request for name and address to every reply card, so each response also reveals what the buyer wants. Use these answers to build segments for later mailings. A list built from stated interests produces more relevant follow-ups than a general list. [L1369.121]
- *Older advice (2021-22):* Email follow-up covers both pre-purchase and post-purchase contacts. Lists should be segmented and built before purchase, and different emails should be sent to prospects versus buyers to keep them engaged and raise the chance of completing a transaction. [L1497.005]

### Naming conventions

- Use the naming convention brand name + double slash + segment name (e.g., "e2 // 0-30 Days Engaged"). Apply this consistently across all segments. [L0264.014]
- Prefix all exclude segments with an asterisk (*) so they are instantly recognizable when searching for exclusions to apply in campaigns (example: *e2 // Purchased Last 7 Days). [L0279.001]
- Name a new list after the campaign and its offers (for example, the campaign name plus "10% and 15%"), so that separate lists can be created later for split tests. In Klaviyo, go to Lists & segments > Create list and give the list a name. [L1138.007]

### Core exclude segments

Apply these five core exclude segments to every email campaign: Purchased Last 7 Days (minimum; consider 14 days), Soft Bounce (3+ bounces), Spam Trap, and Spam Keywords. This approach protects deliverability and maintains sender reputation. [L0279.010]

With proper list management and the five core exclude segments applied consistently, a well-maintained email list should achieve over 30% open rate. [L0279.011]

Focus email marketing on deciding whom NOT to send to rather than whom to send to. Proper exclusions for bounces, inactive profiles, and suspicious keywords ensure messages reach engaged audiences and protect your sender reputation. [L0279.009]

#### Purchased Last 7 Days and Purchased Last 14 Days

- Do not send marketing emails to customers within 5-14 days of purchase. This period is perceived as triggering and annoying. Customers need time to receive and be satisfied with their order before you resume marketing to them. [L0279.004]
- Create a Purchased Last 7 Days exclude segment with the condition: placed order at least once in the last 7 days. Adjust timing based on shipping speed. Purpose: prevent triggering customers with marketing immediately after purchase. [L0279.002]

| Shipping speed | Exclude window | IDs |
|---|---|---|
| Customers receive products within 3 days | 5 days | L0279.002 |
| Standard shipping | 7 days | L0279.002 |
| Maximum aggressive purchase exclusion (minimal marketing contact with recent purchasers) | 14 days (Purchased Last 14 Days: placed order at least once in the last 14 days) | L0279.003 |

- Discount-driven segment: when you want minimal marketing contact with recent purchasers, use the 14-day segment. [L0279.003]
- Check flow structures for suppressions and exclusions, such as customers who have already purchased or who are already in another flow. Blueprints list these as key points that are easy to miss. [L0588.006]

#### Soft Bounce

- Create a Soft Bounce exclude segment for email addresses that have bounced at least 3 times, as soft bounces significantly hurt deliverability. In Klaviyo, the condition is "bounced at least three times overall time." [L0279.005]
- Always exclude profiles that have bounced twice or more, using a segment such as *Bounced x2 or more in the Don't send to field. In the example this excluded 62 profiles. This is a stricter campaign-level exclusion than the 3-bounce core segment. [L0864.007]

#### Spam Trap

- Spam traps are email addresses that Internet Service Providers (Google, Yahoo, Outlook) use to monitor sender reputation. ISPs take over dormant accounts inactive for 5-10 years, sign them up to mailing lists, and watch whether senders continue emailing them without engagement. Senders who keep sending to spam traps get blacklisted. [L0279.007]
- Create a Spam Trap exclude segment with these AND conditions together:
  - Viewed Product = zero times
  - Checkout Started = zero times
  - Placed Order = zero times
  - Opened Email = zero times
  - Clicked Email = zero times
  - Cannot Receive Marketing = true

  Optional: add "subscribed more than 14 days ago" to exclude brand-new inactive profiles and focus on established bot/fake accounts. Profiles automatically exit this segment upon any engagement action. [L0279.006]
- Do not use "active on site" conditions in the spam trap segment, as bots and fraudulent accounts can appear active on site even without genuine engagement. Base spam trap detection on actual engagement behavior: zero emails opened AND zero emails clicked AND zero products viewed AND zero checkouts started AND cannot receive marketing. [L0279.012]

#### Spam Keywords

Exclude emails containing these keywords by creating a segment with "Email contains [keyword]" conditions joined by OR. These patterns indicate non-personal or system accounts that should never receive marketing. [L0279.008]

| Keyword group | IDs |
|---|---|
| support, help@, reply@, nobody, admin, marketing, server, test, spam, subscribe, unsub, job, billing, promotion, careers, contact, service, customer, editor, finance, ftp, info, helpdesk, news, media, relations, investor | L0279.008 |

#### Purchase exclusions for overlapping segments

- Without exclusions, one person can appear in several overlapping segments and receive conflicting or repeated messages. For example, when emailing customers who bought once, exclude customers who bought twice from that audience. *(Older advice, 2021-22.)* [L0603.021]

### Cleaning fake and unengaged subscribers

- Invalid or misspelled email addresses trigger a self-correction: each send attempt bounces, and after 3 or more soft bounces, your soft bounce segment excludes them from future campaigns. This automatic removal continuously improves list hygiene and sender reputation without requiring manual cleaning. [L0279.013]
- Clean fake newsletter subscribers in Klaviyo without double opt-in (double opt-in would drop most real subscribers): [L0971.037]
  1. Once a week, delete obviously fake contacts.
  2. Once a month, run a report in Klaviyo for contacts who have not engaged with emails in the past 30 or 60 days.
  3. Delete those contacts.
- Build an exclusion segment for unengaged profiles at one struggling provider: [L0864.005]
  1. Go to Audience > Lists & segments and click Create segment.
  2. Name it with a single asterisk at the start, for example *Yahoo Unengaged, and avoid multiple asterisks.
  3. Under Definition, choose What someone has done (or not done).
  4. Set Person has > Opened Email zero times between 15 and 365 days ago, and add the same condition for Clicked Email, so the profile has neither opened nor clicked in that window.
  5. Add an AND filter on the profile property email that contains yahoo.
  6. Check the number under Preview details before saving (the example preview showed about 2,446 profiles).
  7. Click Create segment to save it.
- In Klaviyo's Create segment screen, the Segment builder offers a Define with AI option next to the manual Definition builder. The manual route starts with the Definition field, where you choose a condition such as What someone has done (or not done) and then add Person has filters. [L0864.023]
- In a campaign's Recipients step, the Don't send to (optional) field takes exclusion segments, and the Estimated recipients count updates after you add them. The Skip recently emailed profiles option uses Smart Sending, which skips profiles emailed in the past 16 hours by default; this window can be changed in Account Settings. [L0864.006]
- Check the Estimated recipients count in the campaign's Recipients step after adding each exclusion, because it shows how many profiles remain after include and exclude segments. In the example the count was about 2,012 after excluding the bounced and Yahoo unengaged segments. [L0864.028]
- Put the exclusion segment into the Don't send to field of every new campaign, and share it with the team so every email uses it automatically. This keeps the fix in place without needing a manual check each time. [L0864.022]

### Send-to-engaged strategy

- Three-part sending strategy:
  1. Send to engaged subscribers first.
  2. Tailor segments by product or behavior interests.
  3. Exclude unengaged audiences. Most important is knowing who not to send to.

  [L0264.011]
- Segment by date range to control costs. Create audience segments by date range (for example, July-August cohort, August-September cohort). Send the same email campaigns to each segment separately. Klaviyo charges per unique recipient per campaign, so segmented sends cost less than one large campaign. This keeps you on the $150/month plan instead of scaling to $5,000/month. Once scaled up to high-volume pricing, it is difficult to scale back down, so plan segmentation from the start. [L0263.041]

### Avoiding overlap and conflicting messages

- Split the customer list into segments so that different customer groups receive different emails. Segmenting lets one email account serve several audiences without sending the same message to everyone. [L0337.042]
- *Older advice (2021-22):* Do not send all subscribers the same messages from one large list. Split the total list into targeted segments that each receive their own email flow based on actions taken on the site, such as which product was bought or which content page the person opted in on. [L0493.010]
- *Older advice (2021-22):* Emails sent to a segment should be highly relevant to the opt-in point or purchase that placed the subscriber there. Contextual, targeted flows are where the strongest email returns come from. [L0493.011]
- Before sending a segmented message: [L0603.022]
  1. Define the exact group the message is for.
  2. Check whether that group overlaps with another segment.
  3. If it does, add an exclusion for the overlapping segment.
  4. Confirm that the audience now matches the intended message.

### Audience sync to ads

- In Klaviyo, sync an email list or segment to a Facebook custom audience. Subscribers are then shown ads even when they do not open email. [L1204.018]
- Use invisible lists, which are never sent email, only for ad targeting. Keeping them apart from communication lists keeps targeting precise. [L0757.007]

## Welcome Series and New Subscriber Nurture

### Welcome flow structure

- The welcome series is the first automated sequence a new subscriber receives. It is triggered by a sign-up through the pop-up or footer newsletter form, for example a pop-up app such as Wheelio. [L0606.003, L0605.019]
- In Clavio, the series is triggered when a visitor subscribes to the chosen list, such as Newsletter, through the sign-up pop-up. The first email goes out immediately, and later emails follow on set delays. [L0634.034]
- In Klaviyo, the series starts automatically for each person who joins through a sign-up form, so the first email goes out right after subscription. [L0573.033]
- The three-day structure of Source C:
  1. Day 1, welcome email, sent immediately after subscription. Keep it short and simple: thank the subscriber, explain that the newsletter keeps them updated with promotions and offers, and include the brand's social media links. Also send an SMS if budget allows. [L0307.003]
  2. Day 2, brand introduction email, sent 24 hours later. Introduce the brand and go deep on product benefits, technology, design, craftsmanship, and what differentiates you from competitors. For electronics, explain technology benefits. For jewelry, highlight design, designer experience, and handmade quality. For any product, explain why it is worth buying. [L0307.003]
  3. Day 3, user-generated content email. Include a natural product photo (not a professional one) and a text testimonial in this form: "We got an awesome comment on our Facebook/product page: [Customer name] faced [problem], tried our product, [their positive feedback]." This adds social proof without video. [L0307.003]
- The welcome flow order for a community-style brand:
  1. Tell new subscribers they are joining a community, family or club.
  2. Offer a welcoming gift.
  3. If you have a collection, add a collection block after the welcome content.
  4. Add your brand story in the footer. [L0604.002]
- A pop-up welcome flow with a 5-10% welcome kit discount:
  1. Trigger the flow when someone signs up through the pop-up or the footer newsletter form.
  2. Send the first email straight away: welcome them to the community, family or club, give a welcome kit discount of 5-10%, add a link to a collection, and close with a short brand story in the footer.
  3. If the email is not opened after one day, send the same email again with the same offer.
  4. Keep sending emails every 2-3 days, not several times a day, until the subscriber opens an email or buys.
  5. Make the content educational: address the customer's use case and pain points, and avoid hard sales pitches such as "buy now" repeated in every email.
  6. End each email with at most a one-line pitch, such as checking out the collection. [L0605.019]
- The welcome flow has three emails with a 10% storewide discount. The deadline gets shorter in each one: last 3 days, then 48 hours, then 24 hours. The same discount code is used in all three emails. [L0606.010]
- Some sellers run longer introduction sequences of 40 to 50 emails. Choose the length you can realistically write and maintain, since a shorter sequence that gets finished is more useful than a longer one that is abandoned. [L0523.136]
- Stated lengths for welcome or introduction sequences differ by source. Use the length that fits your capacity:

| Source | Length | Note |
|---|---|---|
| Source C, three-day structure | 3 emails | Day 1, Day 2, Day 3 [L0307.003] |
| Source C, three-email flow with 10% offer | 3 emails | Shortening deadlines [L0606.010] |
| Source C, Clavio setup | 2 emails | Email 1 and reminder email 2 [L0634.007, L0634.009] |
| Source C, Klaviyo setup | 3 emails | Thanks, bestsellers, follow-social [L0477.018] |
| Source C, soap-opera sequence | 5 emails | Once per day [L0523.113] |
| Source C, trust series before a higher-priced ask | 5 emails | Story, problems, solutions [L0552.039] |
| Source C, long introduction sequences | 40 to 50 emails | Optional [L0523.136] |
| Source E, welcome flow with offer clock | 5 emails | Offer clock 0 to 4 hours [L1470.020] |
| Source A, 2021 (older) | 3 to 4 emails | Introduce business and expectations [L0801.126] |
| Source A, welcome flow before broadcast | About 4 to 5 emails | Then main broadcast list [L1076.076] |
| Source B, 2021-22 (older) | 30-day master flow | Covers pre- and post-purchase contacts [L0493.018] |

- The first emails must bond the subscriber with your Attractive Character. A boring first email usually means the subscriber stops opening your messages. [L0523.112]
- Place your most emotional story early in the welcome sequence to build a bond with new subscribers before pitching them. Readers who connect with the story tend to open and buy more. [L0378.092]
- The second email of a store welcome series should show bestsellers, active deals or promotions, and customer reviews or testimonials so new subscribers can judge product quality. It can also explain the store's mission and values and how the products match them. Keep it focused on buying decisions rather than on follow-up actions. [L1297.042]
- The third email should build the relationship, not push a sale. Invite subscribers to follow the social accounts or join a loyalty program for rewards, and link useful help such as product guides, size charts, or a customer service phone line so they can buy with confidence. Put the social follow request last, and only include it if the store actually has social accounts. [L1297.043]
- The last welcome email shifts from brand talk to product. A best-sellers block shows what the discount buys. Use this switch when the message moves to the final deadline, so the subscriber sees products instead of another plea. Only show items that are in stock. [L0606.039]
- Sample flow with timed steps: a Time Delay step sets both the gap and the send time between emails. Email one goes out on Day 0, a Wait 3 days step puts email two on Day 3 at 12:00 PM, and a Wait 4 days step puts email three on Day 7 at 12:00 PM. A noon send is a weak fit for welcome emails, so set the delay steps to send around 8 PM instead. [L1470.027]

### Timing and spacing

- Trigger and first send:
  - Shopify: insert a 1-minute wait after the email subscription trigger so processing finishes before conditional checks run. [L0201.011, L0201.005]
  - General, older (Source A, 2021): the first email after someone subscribes or asks about a product usually goes out after a delay of about 5 to 10 minutes. [L1090.114]
  - Use the 1-minute wait in Shopify automations and the 5-10 minute delay as the general reference. [L0201.011, L1090.114]
- Send time: schedule welcome emails for around 8 PM rather than noon. Lunchtime sends were judged a poor fit for this series, so avoid a 12 PM send time after a multi-day wait. [L1470.022]
- Gap between welcome emails: several sources differ, so choose by offer deadline:
  - Second email moves from a 3-day delay to a 1-day delay, because a longer gap lets the buyer lose interest. [L0573.019, L0607.021]
  - Email spacing of about 24 hours: email 1 on signup, email 2 about 24 hours later, email 3 about 48 hours later. For a three-day offer this keeps each email's deadline true, since 48 hours remain after one day and 24 hours after two. [L0606.036]
  - Lead-magnet flow: wait 24 hours between each further email. [L1076.011]
  - Soap-opera sequence: five emails sent once per day after signup. [L0523.113]
  - Ongoing frequency: every 2-3 days, not several times a day, until the subscriber opens an email or buys. [L0605.019]
- Bestsellers and social follow-up in Klaviyo: the bestsellers, offers and reviews email is sent 3 days after signup. The follow-us-on-social-media email is then sent 4 days after that, so the social ask is the last message. [L0477.037, L1297.019]
- Source ambiguity: one Klaviyo procedure labels the 3-day wait as "Email 1 is sent after 3 days' wait" and then lists the bestsellers email as Email 2 after a 4-day wait, which conflicts with the 3-day and 4-day timing above. Read the 3-day wait as the delay before the bestsellers email and the 4-day wait as the delay before the social email. [L0477.018, L0477.037, L1297.019]
- The 30-day master flow spreads early emails in the first days, for example Day 0, Day 2, Day 5 for prospects, and Day 1, Day 7, Day 21 for first-time buyers. [L0493.018]
- Offer deadlines and timing are covered in the discount section below. [L0606.010, L1470.020]

### Discount, deadline and code logic

- Offer values used by the sources, by setup:

| Setup | Offer | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Shopify welcome email example | Save 10 (example code) | [L0201.005] |
| Welcome flow for new subscribers, three-email series | 10% storewide | [L0606.010] |
| Klaviyo prebuilt welcome series | 10% off, static coupon such as welcome_10 or WELCOME10 | [L0573.014, L0573.018, L0607.022] |
| Clavio welcome series | 5 dollars off storewide, code SAVE5 | [L0634.007, L0634.008, L0634.009] |
| Lead-magnet follow-up flow, first coupon | 5% off | [L1076.011] |
| Welcome kit for pop-up subscribers | 5-10% | [L0605.019] |
| Source E sitewide welcome email | 10% off sitewide | [L1470.016] |
| SMS welcome on separate code | 15% VIP code | [L1470.032] |

- Deadline logic:
  - The welcome series deadline is 72 hours, or the expiry date of the coupon or discount code if that is the shorter limit. [L0494.016]
  - Three-email flow: last 3 days, then 48 hours, then 24 hours. [L0606.010]
  - Clavio: email 1 states a three-day deadline; the reminder (email 2) says the offer is available for 48 hours only. [L0634.007, L0634.009]
  - Offer clock in the Source E structure: welcome email with the discount code at 0 hours, bestseller showcase at 48 hours to go, social proof story at 24 hours to go, urgency email at 12 hours to go, and last-chance email when the offer ends in 4 hours. [L1470.020]
  - Only state a deadline you can keep. A true deadline turns interest into action, while a fake one destroys trust. [L0523.119]
- Discount code formula for the welcome email: the subject line names the offer and adds a deadline; the body thanks the new subscriber, shows the discount code in bold on a colored background, states where to use it, and states when it expires. The code is the reason the person subscribed, so it must be easy to find and clearly time-limited. [L0476.014]
- Examples from the same formula: subject "Your $5 welcome code ends Friday", with the code and a Shop now button; and subject "Your 48 hours to claim $5 off start now", with a short thank-you line, the code, and a link to the best-selling collection. [L0476.014]
- Separate codes per channel: give each welcome channel its own discount code, such as a 10% sitewide code for email and a 15% VIP code for SMS. Separate codes show which channel brings redemptions and make the offers easy to tell apart at checkout and in reporting. [L1470.032]
- Automatic application: before saving a discount link, confirm that the discount is set to apply automatically at checkout, so the customer never has to type the code. Use the link itself as the test, since the preview in Klaviyo does not make discount links clickable. [L1470.034]
- Use the same discount code in all emails of one flow. [L0606.010]
- Offer and reward messaging in the Clavio reminder: the discount is valid until the stated time, and the subscriber can still load the cart after that. [L0634.009]
- Newer and older discount advice: the 5% first coupon in the lead-magnet flow is from Source A, undated, and the 5-10% welcome kit is from Source C, undated; both apply to different flow types. [L1076.011, L0605.019]

### Filters: buyers, abandoners and channels

- Audience for the discount: limit the audience to new subscribers who have never placed an order, so buyers do not receive the new-customer discount again. [L0573.033]
- Klaviyo audience setup: select the audience of new sign-ups who have never purchased, then choose a static coupon. [L0573.014]
- Klaviyo prebuilt flow: the prebuilt welcome flow branches between subscribers who have never ordered and customers who have ordered at least once. Customers with at least one order see a separate flow path, so the template covers both groups. [L0607.019, L0573.015]
- Beginners should start with the prebuilt Klaviyo welcome flow. [L0573.015]
- Source E flow filter: add the filter "Placed Order zero times since starting this flow". Anyone who buys is removed from the welcome flow and stops receiving its emails, so the flow only targets new customers. [L1470.004]
- SMS split: add a Conditional Split with the condition "Is not consented to receive SMS". Put the SMS message on the No branch, which means subscribers who did consent to SMS, and let the Yes branch carry only the email path. [L1470.005]
- Shopify abandoned-cart exclusion: exclude customers tagged "abandoned cart" from the welcome automation because they enter a separate abandoned cart automation. Sending both sequences creates duplicate emails and wastes effort. [L0201.009]
- Shopify rules used in the automation: the first rule is Customer > Tags > does not include > abandoned cart, and the second rule is Customer > Amount spent > equals > 0. [L0201.005]
- Pop-up trigger: the welcome flow is triggered for people who enter their email in the pop-up installed on the store. It is the first automated sequence a new subscriber receives. [L0606.003]
- Klaviyo newer flow experience: the pre-built welcome flow can be used with the newer experience. Beginners should start with this pre-built flow. [L0573.015]

### Platform setups

#### Shopify

Create a welcome email automation (Shopify Email):

1. Open Shopify admin > Apps > Email. [L0201.005]
2. Click Create automation. [L0201.005]
3. Select Create custom automation (top right). [L0201.005]
4. Pick the trigger "Customer subscribed to email marketing". [L0201.005]
5. Click + to add a step. [L0201.005]
6. Choose Action. [L0201.005]
7. Select "Wait for a set amount of time". [L0201.005]
8. Set the duration to 1 minute. [L0201.005, L0201.011]
9. Add the next step. [L0201.005]
10. Choose Condition. [L0201.005]
11. Create the first rule: Customer > Tags > does not include > abandoned cart. [L0201.005, L0201.009]
12. Add a second rule: Customer > Amount spent > equals > 0. [L0201.005]
13. Add a step. [L0201.005]
14. Choose Action. [L0201.005]
15. Go to Installed app actions > Email > Send marketing email. [L0201.005]
16. Click Select template. [L0201.005]
17. Pick the "Welcome with discount" template. [L0201.005]
18. Click Select. [L0201.005]
19. Write the subject as "welcome to Source A merch, discount code inside". [L0201.005]
20. Verify the From field. [L0201.005]
21. Write the body: "Hey, we're so happy you're here. Enjoy this discount on your first purchase." [L0201.005]
22. Pick a discount code from the dropdown (example: save 10). [L0201.005]
23. Send a test email to yourself. [L0201.005]
24. Click Save. [L0201.005]
25. If prompted to select the discount code again, do so. [L0201.005]
26. Click + to add an internal notification. [L0201.005]
27. Choose Action. [L0201.005]
28. Keep Shopify selected. [L0201.005]
29. Pick Send internal email. [L0201.005]
30. Enter your email as the recipient. [L0201.005]
31. Set the subject to "new lead - [add variable: created at]". [L0201.005]
32. Write the message: "new lead created - [add variable: customer email]". [L0201.005]
33. At the top, rename from "custom marketing automation" to "welcome email". [L0201.005]
34. Click Turn on workflow. [L0201.005]
35. Click Turn on. [L0201.005]

Shopify welcome email template, with an attached discount and optional content:

```
In Shopify, create a welcome email template with the subject line 'welcome to Source A merch, discount code inside'. The body should say 'Hey, we're so happy you're here. Enjoy this discount on your first purchase.' Attach a discount code (for instance, save 10) with a button that lets customers apply it at checkout. You can optionally include bestsellers or additional promotional content below the main offer.
```

#### Klaviyo

Welcome series, Source C setup (Klaviyo, newer flow):

1. Open Flows, open the welcome series, click Get started, and choose the newsletter list. [L1297.018]
2. Use the "thanks for signing up" trigger. [L1297.018]
3. Turn Smart sending off and UTM tracking on. [L1297.018, L0607.022]
4. Build the first email from a template you can edit: change the logo, set sizes, edit on mobile first, and set the title, product name, product image, and welcome text. [L1297.018]
5. Add the offer and the discount code, highlight the code, set a button labelled "Shop now" that links to the product page, and use the brand color. [L1297.018]
6. Save the template as your store template so later emails reuse it. [L1297.018]
7. Set the email from draft to live. [L1297.018]

Klaviyo welcome series, Source C setup (three emails):

1. Create the flow with a trigger for the newsletter list (get started > choose list). [L0477.018]
2. Email 1 (thanks for signing up): use a template, change the logo, make the mobile version look good, write the headline and body from the prepared text, include the discount code, add a "Shop now" button linking to the product page, use the brand color and social links (delete the section if there are none), and set the background. Save it as your store template. [L0477.018]
3. Email 1 is sent after a 3-day wait, as stated in the source. [L0477.018]
4. Email 2 (bestsellers): after a 4-day wait, introduce bestsellers, deals, reviews or testimonials, and the store's mission and values. [L0477.018]
5. Email 3 (follow us on social media): highlight the loyalty program or social accounts, and resources such as product guides, size charts, or customer service contact. Keep it closed or off if there are no social links. [L0477.018]
6. Set each email from draft to live, then turn the welcome series on. [L0477.018]

Welcome series with the Klaviyo prebuilt template, step by step:

1. Open Flows and click Get started on the Welcome series. [L0607.022]
2. Select the audience of new sign-ups who have never purchased and click Next. [L0607.022]
3. Select a static coupon, enter the code (for example WELCOME10), and set the value to 10 percent off. [L0607.022]
4. Review the prebuilt flow; the first email is sent immediately after sign-up. [L0607.022]
5. Replace the first email's template with a text-based template and edit the subject line, preview text, sender name, and body copy. [L0607.022]
6. Set the second email's delay from 3 days to 1 day and click Save. [L0607.022, L0607.021]
7. Replace the second email with a template from Klaviyo's template library and edit the messaging. [L0607.022]
8. Add a button linking to the product page URL. [L0607.022]
9. Click Review and turn on, and change each email from Draft to Live. [L0607.022]
10. Check that Smart Sending is off and UTM tracking is on for every email. [L0607.022]
11. Click Exit. [L0607.022]

Klaviyo setup, short form: Flows > Welcome series > Get started (Klaviyo template). Select the audience of new sign-ups who have never purchased, choose a static coupon such as welcome_10 with 10 percent off, then review and set each email to Live. [L0573.014]

Changing a message in the Klaviyo template library: click Change on a message and choose a template for the event, then adapt the copy. [L0573.019]

Faster build by cloning:

1. In Klaviyo, open Flows and search for "welcome". [L1470.001]
2. Pick the live flow with the highest revenue. [L1470.001]
3. Click Clone on that flow. [L1470.001]
4. Rename the copy so it names the new sign-up source and offer. [L1470.001]
5. If the copy does not appear, refresh the page, since large flows take a moment to duplicate. [L1470.001]
6. Open the clone and edit it. [L1470.001]

Go-live checks for a welcome flow:

1. Confirm you are in the correct client Klaviyo account. [L1470.023]
2. Check that the trigger, the flow filter, the conditional split, and the SMS and email steps are all set. [L1470.023]
3. Turn off the client's other welcome flows so two flows do not send to the same subscribers. [L1470.023]
4. Use Review and Turn On to switch the flow live. [L1470.023]
5. Notify the client that the flow is live. [L1470.023]

Do not leave the client's old welcome flows running alongside the new one, because subscribers would receive duplicate messages. Confirm the old flows are switched off before going live. [L1470.024]

#### Clavio

Welcome series setup in Clavio:

1. Open Flows and click Get started on the Welcome series. [L0634.007, L0476.009]
2. Select the Newsletter list and click Continue. [L0634.007, L0476.009]
3. Leave the trigger as "Subscribes to newsletter", so the first email sends immediately. [L0476.009]
4. Open the first email with the three-dot menu and click Edit. Set a name (example: welcome gift), a subject line that states the $5 offer with a deadline (example: last three days), a sender name with the store name, and the store email as sender address. [L0476.009, L0634.007]
5. Click Change template, choose Drag and drop, pick a layout, and click Use template. [L0476.009]
6. Click the logo, choose Replace, and upload the logo. If it is too large, set Max width to 100. [L0476.009, L0634.007]
7. Replace the main image with a product photo. Set the button to "Shop now" and link it to the product page URL. [L0476.009]
8. Paste the copy. Keep the first name placeholder as it is. Make the discount code bold and give it a background color so it stands out. [L0476.009]
9. Replace the second image, change the link to the store URL, and add social media links if available. [L0476.009]
10. Remove the address and organization name. Keep the unsubscribe button. [L0476.009, L0634.008]
11. Click Save, check the preview on desktop and mobile, then click Done. [L0476.009]
12. Click Draft and set to live. In the email settings, set Smart sending to off and UTM tracking to on, then click Done. [L0476.009, L0634.007]
13. Open the second welcome email, change its timer from three days to one day after the first email, and set a subject that states the offer is only available for 48 hours. [L0476.009, L0634.007]
14. Once the first email is finished, save it as a reusable template with the three-dot menu, Manage template, and Save as template, so the logo and images do not need to be replaced each time. [L0476.009, L0634.007]

Clavio setup, condensed: Open Flows and click Get started on the Welcome series. Select the Newsletter list and click Continue. Edit email 1 (sent immediately after sign-up) with a subject that states the 5 dollar offer storewide and mentions a three-day deadline. Choose a drag-and-drop template, set logo max width to 100 if it is too large, and replace images with product images. Save the email, set Smart sending off, UTM tracking on, and set it live. Change the delay of email 2 from three days to one day after. Edit email 2 with a reminder subject such as five dollars off storewide for only 48 hours. Save the design as a reusable template via Manage template > Save as, so later emails reuse the logo and images. [L0634.007]

### Templates and copy

Welcome email for the Klaviyo welcome series, to adapt to your brand:

```
Subject line: "A welcome gift for you"
Preheader: "Surprise, a special offer awaits you"
Body: "Thanks for signing up, it means a lot to us. We are one of the best in the business of [your product category, e.g. watches]. Browse through our [collection or product count] collection and enjoy your welcome gift."
Discount line: "Use code welcome_10 to get 10% off", with the code shown in your brand color.
Button: "Shop now", linked to the product page of your first product.
Use the first-name personalization field (Add personalization > first name) where the name appears, and replace the sender name with a real person's name. Adjust the font to match your brand font.
```
[L0573.018]

Welcome email 1 for a store that offers a 5 dollar sign-up discount (replace the bracketed items with your own store details):

```
Subject: '5 dollars off store-wide, last three days'. Sender name: a customer representative first name plus the store name, sent from the store email address. Body: 'Firstly, thanks for signing up. It means a lot to us. We are one of the best in the business of [your product category], and we take this seriously. As a measure of our gratitude, we are giving you a 5 dollar welcome discount. Browse through our collection of [your products]. Use the code SAVE5 during checkout. Remember, the discount vanishes in three days, so better hurry up. We are waiting for you with a store full of [your products]. Cheers, [customer representative first name], [store name], [job title: customer service representative]. Unsubscribe link kept; address and organization name removed.'
```
[L0634.008]

Welcome email 2, reminder with 48 hours left:

```
Subject: '5 dollars off store-wide, only 48 hours left'. Body: 'Hey [first name], relationships aren't easy, we're sensitive folks. You ignoring our welcome gift feels like someone leaving us on read. We are really proud of our collections of [your product], and they really stand out. Our [product name] will steal the show without exception. This is your chance to save while you grab these. Use code SAVE5 during checkout. The discount is valid until tomorrow. You can still load up your cart after tomorrow, but who likes regrets? See you soon. Cheers, [customer representative first name]. Bold and background color on the discount code.'
```
[L0634.009]

Welcome email, Source E template (under 200 words, one clear offer line, one button):

```
Welcome aboard, {FIRST_NAME}. Enjoy 10% off sitewide with code {DISCOUNT_CODE}. Click the button below to shop, and the discount applies automatically at checkout. Keep the entire email under 200 words, with a clear offer line and one button.
```
[L1470.016]

Welcome email structure from Source C, for the welcome email formula, see the discount section. [L0476.014]

Email 1 structure (soap opening, set the stage):
- Pattern: a warm welcome from the character that sets expectations about frequency and promises a valuable gift or lesson soon. The formula is: introduce yourself, share one personal milestone that shows your credibility, state the send schedule, and tease tomorrow's email in a P.S. with its subject line. Subscribers know what to expect and have a reason to open the next message. [L0523.115]
- Example 1, subject "Welcome: Day 1 of 5". Body: "Hi, I am Sam. I started my first online shop from my kitchen table. Tomorrow I will share the checklist I used to get the first 100 orders. P.S. Look for the subject line Day 2 of 5: The checklist." [L0523.115]
- Example 2, subject "Day 1 of 5: A quick hello from me". Body: "Welcome to the group. I will send one short email each day for the next five days. Tomorrow I will show you the one mistake that cost me my first six months." [L0523.115]

Email 2 structure (high drama and backstory):
- Pattern: open at the emotional peak of a story, then rewind to the events that led there, stop at the wall you hit, and leave the answer for the next email. The reader's situation should mirror the backstory. Starting in the middle of a dramatic moment hooks attention faster than starting at the beginning. [L0523.116]
- Example 1, subject "Day 2 of 5: The night my account got frozen". Body: "I stared at the locked screen at midnight, knowing rent was due in four days. Two years earlier I had been sure this business was simple. Here is how I got stuck, and why the answer surprised me. Tomorrow I will show you exactly what I did." [L0523.116]
- Example 2, subject "Day 2 of 5: The month I almost quit". Body: "My order count had been flat for ninety days, and my savings were nearly gone. I had tried every tutorial I could find. I will tell you what finally broke the pattern tomorrow." [L0523.116]

Email 3 structure (epiphany):
- Pattern: describe the moment of realization, which often occurs when a simple observation exposes a flaw in the old approach, and connect the new insight directly to the product you sell. End with a link to the offer. The subscriber now sees the offer as the answer to the drama you built. [L0523.117]
- Example 1, subject "Day 3 of 5: The math that changed everything". Body: "I worked out how much I earned per hour compared with a single product that sold hundreds of copies a day. Selling a system once beat selling my time forever. The method behind it is what I want to show you. Here is the link: [link]." [L0523.117]
- Example 2, subject "Day 3 of 5: The tiny detail I had missed". Body: "One day I noticed that my best customers all came from the same place. That observation became my whole strategy. The short training that teaches it is here: [link]." [L0523.117]

Email 4 structure (hidden benefits):
- Pattern: list two or three benefits of your solution that the reader has not thought about, phrase each as "Sure, you will get X, but you will also get Y", and back them with a short story. Then present the offer with a try-before-you-pay option. Non-obvious benefits create a new reason to act and deepen the bond with the character. [L0523.118]
- Example 1, subject "Day 4 of 5: The benefit nobody mentions". Body: "Sure, you will earn more, but you will also get the freedom to take a Tuesday off. Last month I took three days away, and my store kept selling. Normally this course costs 300 USD, but you can take it free and pay only if you find it useful. Sign up here: [link]." [L0523.118]
- Example 2, subject "Day 4 of 5: More than money". Body: "Sure, you will grow your income, but you will also spend more evenings with your family. I did not expect that. The full program is free to try, and you only pay if it is worth it to you." [L0523.118]

Email 5 structure (urgency and call to action):
- Pattern: give a real reason the offer will change or disappear, such as a webinar starting tomorrow or a discount ending. State the price the reader will pay if they wait, then give one clear link. A true deadline turns interest into action, while a fake one destroys trust. [L0523.119]
- Example 1, subject "Day 5 of 5: Last call". Body: "The free-access window closes tonight at midnight. After that the course returns to its full price of 3,000 USD. If you want to try the complete training before paying, grab your place here: [link]." [L0523.119]
- Example 2, subject "Day 5 of 5: Seats are almost gone". Body: "Only ten seats remain for Thursday's live session. After it starts, late entries pay the full fee. Reserve your seat now: [link]." [L0523.119]

Welcome email principle: keep the content educational and avoid hard sales pitches. [L0605.019]

### Soap-opera and story sequences

- Purpose: the first emails after someone joins your list must bond the subscriber with your Attractive Character. A boring first email usually means the subscriber stops opening your messages. Use the first sequence to build an emotional connection. [L0523.112]
- Soap Opera Sequence, five emails sent once per day after signup:
  1. Email 1, set the stage: thank the subscriber, introduce yourself as the character, explain how often emails arrive, and promise a valuable gift or lesson in the next email. [L0523.113]
  2. Email 2, open with high drama: start at the most intense moment, then tell the backstory that led there, stop at the wall you hit, and open a loop for email 3 without giving the answer. [L0523.113]
  3. Email 3, epiphany: reveal the discovery that changed things for you and tie it directly to the offer, with a link. [L0523.113]
  4. Email 4, hidden benefits: list two or three non-obvious benefits of the solution, tell a parable that shows one, and present the offer with a risk-reducing try-before-you-pay offer. [L0523.113]
  5. Email 5, urgency and call to action: give a real reason to act now and end with one clear call to action. [L0523.113]
- Open a loop in each email and close it in the next one, so each email ends with a reason to open the following one. Tease a result or secret without revealing the details, then give the answer later. Repeat this pattern from email to email. [L0523.114]
- The five-email soap sequence is a short version of a longer introduction sequence; see the length table in the structure section. [L0523.136]
- Trust series before a higher-priced ask: send a five-part email series that tells the brand's story, the problems it solved, and how to solve them in the niche. Then make the larger offer once trust is established. [L0552.039]
- Emotional story placement: see the structure section. [L0378.092]

### 30-day master flow

- The first 30 days after a subscriber joins or a customer buys are the most important engagement period, since interest is highest then. Use this period to build the relationship and drive a first purchase (for prospects) or a repeat purchase (for customers). (Older, Source B, 2021-22.) [L0493.017]
- A 30-day master flow is a single automated email series that covers both pre-purchase and post-purchase contacts, regardless of how the person entered the business. It is built so that the early emails carry the most engagement weight. (Older, Source B, 2021-22.) [L0493.018]
  - Example one: a 30-day flow for prospects who opted in via a guide: welcome email on day 0, product story on day 2, first offer on day 5. [L0493.018]
  - Example two: a 30-day flow for first-time buyers: order follow-up on day 1, usage tip on day 7, repeat-purchase offer on day 21. [L0493.018]
- Build the first 30-day master flow this way: start building it right away, add one email every day or every other day, and accept that the complete flow will take a few weeks rather than a single day. (Older, Source B, 2021-22.) [L0493.019]
- Replicate an existing master flow for each new entry point to the email list (older, Source B, 2021-22):
  1. Copy the complete flow. [L0493.020]
  2. Update only the specific emails that need to be more relevant to the new entry point. [L0493.020]
  3. Keep shared early emails, such as the welcome email, unchanged. [L0493.020]
- Integrate introductory emails for the loyalty program, referral program, and customer feedback survey into your existing 30-day new customer welcome sequence, so all new customers receive them automatically. (Older, Source B, 2021-22.) [L0203.012]

### Content-based nurture sequences

- Send automated sequences to subscribers grouped by the content piece or product page they opted in on, with emails leading down the purchase path linked to that content. The first few emails should follow the call to action in that content, such as buying a recommended product. (Older, Source B, 2021-22.) [L0603.020]
  - Example pattern: an opt-in from a guide comparing two products leads to emails that explain the guide's pick and then push that product. [L0603.020]
- Quiz and buyer's guide follow-up flow:
  1. The visitor completes a product quiz. [L1204.021]
  2. A follow-up email sequence sends product recommendations based on the quiz answers. [L1204.021]
  3. The sequence points the visitor to the buyer's guide. [L1204.021]
  4. If the visitor downloads the buyer's guide first, the sequence points them to the quiz instead. [L1204.021]
- Giveaway leads: leads captured by the giveaway funnel go into email nurture sequences that aim to convert them over time. These email flows also generate part of the measured revenue. [L0797.006]
- To launch quickly, duplicate your existing welcome flows or welcome series and run a similar funnel alongside them. [L0797.020]

### Lead-magnet follow-up flows

- Collecting opt-ins without a follow-up email sequence produces leads who never buy. The money from a lead magnet comes from the follow-up, so every opt-in must enter a flow. [L1076.012]
- Build a separate flow for every lead magnet, so each requested item receives its own follow-up sequence. A single flow is enough at the start, and you add more as you create more free items. [L1076.024]
- Lead-magnet email flow in Klaviyo:
  1. Immediately send the requested free item with the download link. [L1076.011]
  2. Wait 24 hours. [L1076.011]
  3. Send a welcome email introducing the company, with a first coupon such as 5 percent off. [L1076.011]
  4. Wait 24 hours between each further email and keep making offers. [L1076.011]
- A new lead should first go through a welcome flow of about four to five emails, after which they move into the main broadcast list. Beginners can start with about one broadcast email a week and increase the frequency as the list grows. [L1076.076]
- Download-delivery emails: in one published benchmark (older, Source B, 2021-22), welcome and thank-you emails that contained a download link for a lead magnet had an 88.7% open rate and a 35.07% click-through rate. Use these figures as a reference point when measuring your own download-delivery emails. [L0571.029]
- The email that delivers a lead magnet is opened by most subscribers, so it can carry a second offer. Use it as a second conversion point, for example to promote a membership discount or a related product. (Older, Source B, 2021-22.) [L0571.030]
- Influencer digital products:
  1. Send a five-email sequence that provides value first. [L0789.038]
  2. Then send the new subscribers a 24-hour promotion with a coupon to convert them into buyers. [L0789.038]

### Ongoing welcome-series handling

- Tag-based and purchase-based exits: keep the welcome flow limited to new subscribers, using the buyer and abandoner filters above, so the flow does not mail people who already bought or who sit in a separate abandoned cart sequence. [L0201.009, L1470.004, L0573.033]
- Use separate codes per channel and check redemption by channel in reporting. [L1470.032]
- Smart sending and tracking: set Smart sending off and UTM tracking on for every welcome email, so the flow sends on the set schedule and shows its traffic in analytics. [L0476.009, L0607.022, L1297.018]
- Saving a reusable store template: save the first email as a template so later emails reuse the logo, images and layout. [L0476.009, L1297.018, L0634.007]
- Reviewing the flow after launch: check each email's delay, the discount link, and the send time, then review opened and clicked results. [L1470.022, L1470.034]
- Duplicate flows: confirm that the old welcome flows are off before switching the new flow on, and notify the client that the flow is live. [L1470.023, L1470.024]
- Discount link test: test the link itself, because the preview in Klaviyo does not make discount links clickable. [L1470.034]
- Ongoing frequency after the welcome series: continue with one message every 2-3 days until the subscriber opens an email or buys. [L0605.019]
- Ongoing sequence after the welcome series: move new leads from the welcome flow into the main broadcast list. [L1076.076]

#### Further details

- Build one autoresponder sequence that delivers the bribe immediately and then follows up on a schedule. Each email is scheduled for a specific day, so email one goes out on day one, email two on day two, and so on. [L0514.018]

## Abandoned Cart and Checkout Recovery (Email)

### Purpose and Priority

- An abandoned checkout occurs when a customer adds products, starts checkout, and does not complete the purchase [L0282.001]. An abandoned cart flow is triggered for shoppers who started checkout but did not complete the order, and it brings them back to the cart they already built [L0606.004].
- Visitors who add to cart and leave without buying waste paid traffic unless you can follow up with them. Automated reminder emails or SMS let you re-contact them hours later at no extra ad cost [L0476.001].
- Roughly 7 to 8 out of 10 shoppers who add products to their cart leave without buying, so an abandoned-cart sequence reaches a large share of site visitors [L0572.006].
- Recovering about 25% of abandoned carts through an email sequence is cited as a realistic result to aim for once the flow is optimized [L0572.007].
- Abandoned cart recovery is among the cheapest ways to win back shoppers who added items to their cart [L1251.039].
- Improving the abandoned-cart flow is one of the fastest ways to see results in an online store, because it recovers sales that already had strong intent. Gains can show as lower cost per lead, better traffic quality and a higher conversion rate [L1208.020].
- A recovered buyer can become a repeat customer who leaves reviews, refers others and eventually becomes a brand advocate. This long-term value justifies the extra effort in recovery sequences [L1208.013].
- Set up the abandoned cart email sequence before any other optimization. Use it to learn why visitors leave, and avoid discounts as the first lever; discounts should be strategic and paired with something of higher value [L0575.027].
- The abandoned cart sequence should be set up before traffic is scaled [L0430.013].
- Older advice (2018): set up the abandoned cart email sequence in Klaviyo from day one, before any traffic campaigns run [L1085.004].
- Older advice (2021): once the store is established, run abandoned cart follow-up as an automated email flow, for example one built in Klaviyo [L0671.020].
- Older advice (2021): Klaviyo is used to set up abandoned cart emails with customized messages and urgency. Abandoned carts can turn into sales in the days after a visit, so configure these early [L1279.047].
- Older advice (2021-22): an abandoned cart email sequence is an automated message sent through the email autoresponder when a shopper leaves a cart without buying [L1298.031].
